- **Residual:** the `aws-kms` and `vault-transit` convention strings are repeated in `@infrabyx/tenancy` beside the adapters' `createTenantKey`; a drift between them fails closed (key not found), not open.

### 3.78 Scoped break-glass and more grantee kinds (PR-LDC-07, PR-LDC-08 (c), R1-D-75)

- **E (break-glass used beyond what the incident needs):** Control: a grant may carry a scope (one tenant, its modules, read or read-write) that only narrows it; the kernel checks the active grant's scope (definer function `breakglass_scope`) before each transaction and refuses another tenant, a platform context and a write under `read`; the database refuses the same in `breakglass_active` and `breakglass_touch`. Under a module list every statement is tokenized (comments and literals dropped, quoted names kept) and any name that is a database schema outside the list is refused, as are statements that could reach a module unnamed (search path, `set_config`, `SET`, `DO`, `PREPARE`/`EXECUTE`, `COPY`, `CALL`, `*_to_xml`).
- **R (an out-of-scope attempt hidden):** Control: each refusal is ledgered in the tenant touched in its own transaction (`security.breakglass.scope_refused`), even when the caller catches the error; without an audit sink a scoped grant is refused.
- **Residual:** the guard is lexical and fails closed (a name that is both an alias and a schema is refused); unqualified names resolve only through the default search path, which holds no tenant data by convention; a module's own view or definer function that reads another module is that module's responsibility. The new grantee kinds carry the same 90-day, second-approver, read-only controls as before (R1-D-29).

### 3.79 Tenant-consented support access (PR-LDC-08 (a), (b), PR-LDC-20; #104; R1-D-76)

- **E (support staff reading a tenant without its knowledge):** Control: `PLT_SUPPORT` sees nothing in a tenant without a live grant (`IPB-TRUST-003`, checked in every transaction); the tenant's ORG_OWNER or ORG_ADMIN consents first, and read-write also needs a second, different one (check constraints: consenter ≠ requester, second approver ≠ both); at most 24 hours, revocable at once, module-scoped, read-only by default; a read-write grant cannot list `tenancy` or `audit`, so it cannot touch its own record or the ledger.
- **S (a product-defined role posing as platform staff):** Control: only the contracts role `PLT_SUPPORT` is honoured; a context holding it holds nothing else and is never a platform context, the platform tenant, impersonation or break-glass (R1-D-74).
- **R (a read hidden from the tenant):** Control: a support principal reads only through `SupportReads`; each read is ledgered and recorded in `tenancy.support_read`, written only by a definer function for the principal's own grant (application roles hold SELECT only); refusals are ledgered.
- **I (cross-tenant):** Control: grants and reads are tenant rows under forced RLS, the grant lookup bound to tenant and principal.
- **Residual:** under read-write, what a mutation reads inside its own transaction is covered by that mutation's ledger entry, not a separate read record.

### 3.80 Refresh-token rotation and reuse detection (`refreshSession`; PR-LDC-03, R1-D-77)

- **S and E (a stolen refresh token replayed):** Control: rotation on every refresh; a spent token presented again revokes the whole session before the refusal is returned (the revocation store first, so it holds even if the IdP call fails, then the IdP session). On Zitadel the adapter's hash ledger also refuses every later token of the family and revokes it at Zitadel (RFC 7009).
- **I (tokens in logs or errors):** Control: no refusal, detail or meta carries a token (a contract case asserts it); the Zitadel ledger keeps SHA-256 hashes only; a Keycloak refresh token is decoded, unverified, only to name the session to revoke, and only after Keycloak has verified it and reported its reuse.
- **D (revocation used to sign someone out):** Control: only a token the IdP verified (Keycloak), or a hash the adapter recorded as spent (Zitadel), triggers a revocation; an unknown or forged token is `refresh_invalid` and revokes nothing.
- **Residual:** the default Zitadel ledger is per replica (a reuse on another replica is refused but revokes nothing until a shared ledger is supplied); without a `sid`, Zitadel revokes the user (stricter); a client's parallel refreshes look like a reuse.

### 3.81 Identity-provider brokers and domain discovery (PR-LDC-09, R1-D-78)

- **S and E (a tenant's broker used to sign in to another tenant, or as another user):** Control: linking only on both IdPs (no account created through a broker; an unknown identity refused): Keycloak's first-login flow `ipb-broker-link-only` (detect the existing account, then re-authentication), restored by `applyRealm`, `trustEmail` off; Zitadel linking on, creation off. A link needs the account's own user to sign in first. A session whose broker belongs to another tenant than its account is refused (`idp_tenant`). Aliases are owned by one tenant; hand-configured IdPs are never taken over.
- **E (the admin account's rights):** Control: `identityBrokers` adds only `manage-identity-providers` and `view-identity-providers` (never `manage-realm`); the flow and the claim are created at deploy time.
- **T (a broker changed without approval):** Control: broker changes are R3 maker-checker requests in the product; the adapter only executes; admin credentials stay in SECRETS.
- **I (client secrets):** Control: the port takes a `secret://` reference only, resolved at the call, sent to the IdP over TLS, never returned by `list`, stored in metadata or logged.
- **SSRF (tenant-supplied URLs):** Control: https only (`IPB-SEC-018` otherwise) and approved through maker-checker; Keycloak fetches documents from its own network position, so its egress should allow the internet only, not internal ranges.
- **Spoofed routing:** Control: only domains the product verified route; one domain to one broker; disabled brokers do not route.
- **Residual:** ownership and domain checks are read-then-write; discovery and the broker list are cached 30 s per replica; Zitadel's link check reads an eventually consistent projection cached 60 s per user; discovery reaches the admin API, so rate-limit it; Keycloak's re-authentication is password-based, so a passkey-only account cannot complete a link.

### 3.82 Company data by CIN or LLPIN (`CompanyDataPort`; PR-LDC-02, R1-D-79)

- **S and T (a forged or malformed identifier reaching the source, or a scraped source):** Control: CIN, LLPIN and company-id schemas refuse anything malformed before any call; IPB-01 forbids MCA21 scraping, and no adapter exists until the owner chooses a licensed source (#102).
- **I (director names and DINs disclosed beyond need):** Control: lookups are INTERNAL and results are public-register data, never logged in full; no PAN is returned (a contract case checks it); fixtures are fictional.
- **T (stale register data taken as current):** Control: every result carries `asOf` and `stale`; a failover cache is labelled, never silent.
- **Residual:** no adapter yet; the source's licence terms and accuracy are assessed when the owner chooses one.

### 3.83 Collection rails and settlement lines (`DisbursementRailPort`, `SettlementLine`; PR-FBX-34, R1-D-80)

- **E (an agent or an unapproved caller presenting debits):** Control: `assertDebitBatch` applies `assertMoneyCall` to every item (an agent is `IPB-SEC-004`; a missing approval, initiator or key is `IPB-UX-004`) and refuses the whole batch on any failure. The product's maker-checker runs before the call; the approvals engine does not consume debit approvals (unlike mandates, R1-D-66), which would be an owner decision.
- **T (a double debit by a retried or edited batch):** Control: one key, one presentation; a repeat in a batch is refused, a retry is a replay, another item under the key is `IPB-PORT-004`; integer paise only.
- **T and R (a forged or mismatched response file):** Control: a file naming an unpresented item, a different amount or a contradicting result is refused whole; unknown return codes are `final`, so nothing is re-presented on an unchecked code; a re-presentation is a new money call with a fresh key and approval. The file's transport authenticity is the adapter's, specified with the sponsor bank.
- **I (account and mandate data):** Control: items, statement and settlement lines are REGULATED and `IN`.
- **Residual:** the return-code table is `[VERIFY]` against the sponsor bank's file specification; no adapter until a sponsor bank and the owner's word.

### 3.84 External-read summary (`AuditService.externalReadSummary`; PR-LDC-10, R1-D-81)

- **I (one tenant's external reads seen by another, or personal data in a report):** Control: read in the caller's context under the ledger's forced RLS (no tenant id is passed); ids, counts and times only, never IP or user-agent HMACs, trace ids, digests or names.
- **E (a grantee reading its own audit trail):** Control: external principals are refused (`IPB-SEC-005`).
- **Residual:** reads are counted, not rows (row counts sit only inside each entry's digest); the summary is as complete as the ledger, whose chain verifier covers tampering.

### 3.85 Setting change history (`config.setting_change`; PR-LDC-17, R1-D-82)

- **T (history rewritten):** Control: no UPDATE or DELETE grants for `app_rw` or `app_ro`; each row names its ledger entry, whose before and after digests must match the row's values, and the ledger is hash-chained and anchored.
- **S (a change attributed to someone else):** Control: the insert policy requires `actor_id = app.actor_id`, and rows are written only inside the kernel transaction that changes the value.
- **I (another tenant's or a platform key's history):** Control: forced RLS on `app.tenant_id`; platform tiers only in a platform context; USER history only for its owner; TENANT and ENTITY history needs a changer, owner, admin or auditor role; agents and external principals refused.
- **Residual:** the table holds CONFIDENTIAL values with no purge and is not yet in the tenant export maps; its retention, the erasure of USER rows and its export are OWNER-QUESTIONS #110.

### 3.86 Breached-password list on Keycloak (PR-LDC-04, R1-D-83)

- **I (password material sent to a third party):** Control: the list is bundled in the image and read locally by Keycloak's `passwordBlacklist`; no range query and no download at build or run time (the static image test checks it).
- **T (the list swapped or emptied):** Control: the list is in the repository with a SHA-256 checked by `pnpm images:test`, copied read-only (0444) into an image built from a pinned digest.
- **D (realm application fails):** Control: Keycloak refuses a realm naming a missing list, so the failure is loud at deploy; the option is per realm and off by default.
- **Residual:** exact matching after lower-casing (variants pass, though length, history, username and email rules still apply); a static snapshot to refresh as its README describes.

### 3.87 Bureau EMI, the DDE signer trail and not-stampable DDE (PR-FBX-35, R1-D-84)

- **T (a missing instalment read as zero, so FOIR is understated):** Control: `emiPaise` is `null` whenever the bureau gives no usable value, never `0`; the guide tells products to treat `null` as unknown; the contract case requires both an EMI and a `null` from the known subject.
- **R (an executant denies signing a DDE document):** Control: each signed executant's evidence (reference, time, method, ESP id or IP) is read back from NeSL, never taken from the callback; a partial or malformed trail is a vendor error, so a record without evidence is never returned.
- **I (personal data in the trail):** Control: only the party reference, time, method, ESP id and IP are kept; the executant's name is not in the record (tested); the IP is CONFIDENTIAL signing evidence, as for ESIGN.
- **T and E (a stampable instrument registered as not stampable to avoid duty):** Control: the schema refuses `notStampable` with a certificate or duty and refuses a registration with neither; the reason comes from a closed code table (`OTHER` needs a note); NeSL's echo must match; a key replayed with another stamping is refused; the stampability decision is the product's, under its own RULES and maker-checker, and the reason is recorded for audit.
- **Residual:** CIBIL and NeSL field names are stub-shaped (`[VERIFY]`) until the sandboxes exist; whether a document is truly not an instrument stays the product's legal determination.

### 3.88 Rule parameters, publish checkers, as-of evaluation (PR-LDC-21, 22, 25; R1-D-86, R1-D-88)

- **T (a tenant parameter loosens a rule without review):** Control: a parameter's value comes only from a registered setting, changed through settings' maker-checker and history; its type, bounds and allowed values are fixed in the published rule set (itself R4 maker-checker); an out-of-range value makes the decision UNDETERMINED (human queue), never a match; the caller cannot pass a parameter as input.
- **R (which threshold applied to a past decision):** Control: the decision log records each parameter's value and source and, for an as-of evaluation, the instant; `explain` returns them.
- **E (a product role, an agent or platform staff checks a publish):** Control: `publishCheckerRoles` accepts tenant and product roles only and refuses `PLT_`, `AGT_`, `SVC_` and `OPS_` roles at construction; a platform context keeps the platform checkers; maker ≠ checker and the tenant's tighten-only narrowing still apply; every publish is ledgered.
- **D (compilation under load turns decisions UNDETERMINED):** Control: ZEN warms each compiled decision; `prepare` and the executed publish compile ahead of traffic; the cache is bounded (256 entries per version and parameter set).
- **Residual:** as-of evaluation uses the tenant's current parameter values, not those in force at the instant; the decision records which values it used.

### 3.89 XLSX and DOCX exports (PR-LDC-28, R1-D-90)

- **T and I (formula injection in an opened export):** Control: `toXlsx` writes only inline-string, number, boolean and date cells, never a formula; text starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR (headers included) gets an apostrophe; `toCsv` offers the same rule for spreadsheet-bound CSV.
- **T (XML injection):** Control: every text and attribute value is escaped and characters outside XML 1.0 dropped; part names, relationship ids and styles are fixed by the writer; DOCX writes no fields, macros, images or OLE objects; the only external target is an https hyperlink without credentials.
- **D (oversized output, a decompression bomb for the reader):** Control: grid, cell, sheet, block and link limits; uncompressed parts counted against a 64 MiB default cap (at most 1 GiB); the ZIP writer refuses archives that would need ZIP64.

### 3.90 Tenant brand layer (`brand/0002`; PR-LDC-26, R1-D-90)

- **S (spoofing through the email identity):** Control: the from-address domain must be verified (SPF, DKIM, DMARC) through the same NOTIFY hook as packs, at proposal and again at approval; the from-name is single-line plain text without quotes, `@` or angle brackets (no header injection or display-name spoofing); the pack's reply-to and DKIM selector are never carried onto a tenant address; every change needs a second person.
- **S (spoofing through the domain):** Control: a host is unique across pack hosts and every tenant's domain (code and triggers); a domain selects the layer only when the EDGE hook confirms control; the tenant is chosen from `Host` only, never `X-Forwarded-Host`. Residual: the clash check reveals that a host is taken (public DNS information).
- **T (XSS through SVG or the login message):** Control: logos, favicons and SVG letterheads must be sanitiser-written objects (stored tag and matching SHA-256) under the tenant's own key; other letterheads are PDF, PNG or JPEG with a matching stored type; the login message refuses `<`, `>`, character references, control and bidirectional characters, and is rendered as text.
- **I and E (tenant isolation):** Control: `brand.tenant_layer_version` and `brand.tenant_host` have RLS enabled and forced on `app.tenant_id` (rls-lint clean); the only cross-tenant read is the host index from platform-tenant contexts; the executor refuses another tenant's payload; the strict schema and the merge keep the legal entity, terminology, policy set and consent the pack's; versions are immutable.

### 3.91 Tenant notification templates (`notify/0004`; PR-LDC-27, R1-D-90)

- **T and S (HTML injection or phishing through a tenant template):** Control: an allow-list lint with no sanitiser (script, style, iframe, form, input, object, embed, svg, link, base and comments refused; no `on…` attributes; links https, mailto or tel only, checked after decoding references; no credentials in URLs; link hosts limited by `linkHosts` when set; only inline raster images; CSS from a property allow-list; no variables inside attributes); every value escaped in HTML and line breaks stripped from the subject; every change R3 maker-checker with the full text in the checker's diff; digest, base version and lint checked again at activation; security classes keep the catalogue email.
- **I (variable leakage):** Control: only the class's declared `variables` may appear, checked at request, approval and every render; a class that loses a variable fails the delivery closed (`template_invalid`).
- **T (unapproved SMS or WhatsApp wording, another entity's DLT template or sender):** Control: overrides only select a template the product's registry returns for that tenant; sender and entity ids come from the registry; the selection is re-checked at activation and delivery and fails closed when withdrawn.
- **I and T (tenant isolation):** Control: `notify.tenant_template` is tenant-owned under forced RLS and every query also filters on the context tenant; the dispatcher's cross-tenant reader has no grant on it.
- **Residual:** text-only social engineering inside allowed wording relies on the checker; with `linkHosts` unset any https host is allowed (set it to the tenant's verified domains).

### 3.92 Sandbox tenants (`tenancy/0018`, `notify/0005`; PR-LDC-29, R1-D-90)

- **E (a tenant takes itself out of the sandbox):** Control: the BEFORE UPDATE trigger `tenant_sandbox_guard` refuses any change to `sandbox` unless the transaction is platform-scoped and carries the action `tenancy.tenant.sandbox.set`, both set only by the kernel from the verified context (callers cannot `set_config('app.…')`); the only path is `TenantSandbox.apply` after a maker-checker approval (§3.97; a reason, ledgered in the tenant).
- **S (a caller forges `ctx.sandbox`):** Control: `contextFromClaims` never copies it, the kernel replaces it in every transaction with the tenant row's value, joined mutations inherit the ambient transaction's value, and NOTIFY and the AI gateway read it only from `tx.ctx`. Residual: a non-PgKernel kernel (a test fake) passes the caller's value through.
- **Fail closed:** the lookup is a SECURITY DEFINER function bound to `app.tenant_id`; a transaction run as a role without the lookup carries no flag and `isSandboxed` treats it as a sandbox; a failed lookup aborts the transaction. A tenant without a row is not a sandbox (it was never flagged). In-app messages, which never leave the platform, are the stated exception.

### 3.93 Form-layout lint and a shorter approval expiry (PR-LDC-24, PR-LDC-19)

- **Form lint:** no port, adapter, data flow or stored data; `axeFormScan` reuses the existing in-page axe injection under the same CSP assumption (PL5-D-03).
- **E (a tenant lengthens its approval window to keep stale requests alive):** Control: `mc.expiry_hours` is tighten-only, at most 168 hours, changed by ORG_OWNER under R4, and the engine never exceeds the action's platform period nor goes below 1 hour.

### 3.94 Work on a connection after its transaction ended (`PgKernel.ambient`; R1-D-92, security patch)

- **I and T (a call that outlives its transaction reaches another tenant's):** A joined call left running when its ambient mutation ended (a refused concurrent sibling, a call not awaited) kept the `PgTx` of a connection already returned to the pool; its next statements ran in the next transaction on that connection, under another tenant's `app.tenant_id`, and RLS then admitted them for that tenant (reproduced by `ambient-race.int.test.ts` with a one-connection pool). Control: every kernel transaction is marked ended before its commit or rollback, and any statement, unit or settings change after that is refused (`KernelGuardError`), so nothing reaches a returned connection; an ambient mutation waits for every joined call still running and then fails, so it never commits half a call; a joined call that starts after the ambient mutation ended, or whose own unit is over, is refused. Residual: none known for the kernel's own transactions; code outside the kernel that holds a raw pool client is outside this control.

### 3.95 Setting history in the export, and DPDP erasure of USER settings (`config/0004`, `REF-EXPORT-1.5`; #110, R1-D-95)

- **I (another tenant's history in an export):** Control: `config.setting_change` is read in the export worker's tenant context under forced RLS, so an archive carries the tenant's own TENANT, ENTITY, UNIT and USER rows and never PLATFORM, PLAN or BRAND rows (their `tenant_id` is null). Values are CONFIDENTIAL and travel in the signed archive like `setting.value`.
- **T and repudiation (an erasure used to rewrite history):** Control: the history's only delete policy admits USER rows of a person who has a `config.setting_erasure` row in the same tenant; that row is written first, in the same transaction as the deletes and the `settings.user.erase` ledger entry (counts and the request reference, never a value); UPDATE stays ungranted; agents, externals and platform contexts are refused; the callers are a service principal, ORG_OWNER or ORG_ADMIN. Residual: an ORG_ADMIN can erase a person's USER preferences with a reason; the ledger keeps who and why.

### 3.96 Setting scopes below ENTITY (`config/0005`; PR-042, PR-LDC-17, R1-D-95)

- **E (a unit loosens a security setting):** Control: UNIT is allowed only for a key that declares `unit_override` (none of the platform's keys does), stays tighten-only against the tenant, the entity and each outer unit, and needs the key's change role with the unit in the caller's own `ctx.unitIds`.
- **S (a forged unit chain):** `ctx.unitIds` comes from the product's own organisation model, set server-side like `entityId`; at most 8 ids of a fixed alphabet. Residual: the platform does not hold the organisation model, so a product that builds the chain from a request body would let a caller pick a unit's values; the guide says to build it from the verified principal only. UNIT rows follow `app.tenant_id` under RLS like ENTITY rows.

### 3.97 Sandbox changes under maker-checker (#115, R1-D-95)

- **E (one administrator takes a production tenant into the sandbox, silencing its mail):** Control: `TenantSandbox.set` is refused; a change is an R3 request by a PLT_SUPER_ADMIN, approved by a different PLT_SUPER_ADMIN or PLT_SECURITY_OFFICER in a platform context only (a tenant's own checkers cannot decide it, and a tenant's `mc.*` settings do not narrow platform checkers), then applied once by a PLT_SUPER_ADMIN through `ApprovalsEngine.consumeIn` in the `tenancy.tenant.sandbox.set` transaction, so the database guard still holds; the ledger keeps the reason and the request id. The request is visible to the tenant's approvers in their own tenant, which is information about their own tenant only.

### 3.98 PDF export (PR-LDC-28, R1-D-95)

- **No new dependency or data flow.** `toPdf` writes caller text into a PDF with the standard fonts; no font parsing, no images, no JavaScript, forms, attachments, Launch or OpenAction; links are https annotations only. Characters outside WinAnsi are refused, never replaced, so amounts and names are never silently altered.

### 3.99 Embedded fonts and the DOCGEN route for Indic PDFs (#117; R1-D-96, R1-D-97)

- **T (a tampered font in the package):** the four Noto Sans subsets are vendored with their upstream and subset SHA-256 and the upstream commit (`export/fonts/FONTS.md`), published inside the signed and attested package; `ttf.ts` reads only those files, never a caller's font, and parses metrics and the character map only (no outlines, no hinting programs).
- **I and T (the HTML sent to DOCGEN):** `blocksToHtml` escapes every text and attribute, allows https links only, and sets a CSP of `default-src 'none'` (inline styles only), so the renderer fetches nothing and runs no script. The HTML carries the document's data (CONFIDENTIAL by default in `HtmlRenderRequest`); the DOCGEN adapter keeps the port's rules (in-cluster, output to the tenant's STORAGE prefix).

### 3.100 Purge runs and per-record entries (`consent/0005`; PR-FBX-37, R1-D-97)

- **E and repudiation (a live run deletes more than was reviewed):** a live run deletes only the ids its dry run listed, under the unchanged 8-year rule and deletion guard; it needs a dry run of the same tenant (RLS) at most 72 hours old; each record deleted has its own ledger entry (id and purpose only). The run table is insert-only for the application. The dry run's delete runs under the purge's action inside a savepoint that is always rolled back.

### 3.101 The TELEPHONY port (ADR-0007; PR-FBX-38, R1-D-97)

- **I (a recording kept without a disclosure, or kept by the vendor):** `storeRecording` refuses unless the request named a disclosure and the adapter reports it was played; the recording goes to the tenant's STORAGE with a retention and the vendor keeps no copy once stored. **E (an agent places calls):** refused by the request schema and by the contract cases. No adapter exists yet, so no data leaves the platform through this port.

### 3.102 `X-Infrabyx-JWS` (Ecosystem Spec v1.4; M61)

- **S and T (a missing or substituted proof):** a regulated message without exactly one well-formed header is refused before verification; the header value is verified against the pinned key ring with the `iat` window and `jti` replay check, then kept byte for byte. A substituted JWS for another event fails verification over the JCS of this event.

### 3.103 Base image: apt CVE-2026-109518 (waived 30 days; R1-D-99)

- **T (an on-path attacker during apt downloads):** apt is in the Debian 12 base of both platform images but is never run: the runtime fetches no packages, runs as UID 10001 with every capability dropped and a read-only root. Waived until 2026-12-05 (`infra/docker/trivyignore.yaml`) because no `node:24.22.1-bookworm-slim` digest at least 7 days old carries the fixed apt 2.6.2; the waiver goes with the first such digest.

### 3.104 The Gotenberg DOCGEN adapter (`connectors/docgen`; #120, R1-D-100)

- **I and T (HTML that makes the renderer fetch or run something):** the adapter sends one `index.html` and no other file or URL, asks Chromium to fail on console exceptions and not to wait for the network; pages from `blocksToHtml` carry a CSP of `default-src 'none'`. Deploy Gotenberg with no egress (a network policy) and its own `--chromium-deny-list`/`--chromium-allow-list` defaults kept, so a page from any other source still cannot reach out. **S (a forged renderer):** HTTPS to the configured host only (the SSRF guard), optional basic auth from SECRETS. **I (data at rest):** the PDF goes straight to the tenant's STORAGE, CONFIDENTIAL by default; Gotenberg keeps nothing.

### 3.105 Retiring a statutory-capture registration (`audit/0008`; #122, R1-D-102)

- **T and R (a captured table dropped with its rows, or the capture switched off under cover of a retirement):** `retire_capture` runs only for `app_migrate`, only inside a migration applied by the platform runner (its transaction marker and advisory lock), and only on an empty table, counted past row-level security under an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock; writes to the table are refused from then on. The drop guard admits only the drop of that table and its `ipb_capture*` triggers, only in the retiring transaction and only with the table itself; a deferred check fails the commit if the table survives. Every other drop is refused as before. **R:** the retirement is a chained capture entry in the platform chain and each affected tenant's chain; no capture row is removed; `audit.capture_retirement` and `audit.capture_table` stay append-only except for that one sanctioned delete. **E:** the function is SECURITY DEFINER owned by the database owner; it fails closed where its owner cannot read past row-level security.

### 3.106 The tenant-id rule (`tenancy/0019`; PR-FBX-40, R1-D-103)

- **D (a tenant that cannot use a port) and T (a tenant id that could be read as a path):** a tenant id is `tnt_` then lower-case letters and digits, or a ULID; the database refuses any other at creation, and no id it admits contains a separator a storage key could misread. The migration names, and does not change, existing ids that break the rule.

### 3.107 The DISPATCH, COURT_DATA and ODR ports (ADR-0008 to 0010; R1-D-104)

- Interfaces and fakes only: no new data flow leaves the platform until an adapter lands, each with its own threat-model entry. **E (an agent acting outside):** DISPATCH and ODR refuse an agent initiator, as TELEPHONY does. **R:** dispatch proofs carry the document's SHA-256; court data names its source and time and is never treated as a verified order; ODR referrals carry their basis and the disclosure version shown. **I:** addressees, parties, case files and outcomes are CONFIDENTIAL and go only to the tenant's own STORAGE prefix.

### 3.108 Base image: zlib1g CVE-2026-76844 (waived 30 days; R1-D-106)

- **T and D (crafted compressed input):** `zlib1g` is in the Debian 12 base of both platform images, with no fixed package. Node.js uses its own bundled zlib for every compression the app does, and the runtime runs no system tool on untrusted archives (UID 10001, no capabilities, read-only root). Waived until 2026-12-05 (`infra/docker/trivyignore.yaml`); Trivy's record looks misattributed and is re-checked when its database updates.

### 3.110 Base image: zlib1g CVE-2026-85091 (waived 30 days; R1-D-111)

- **T and D (a gzprintf after a write stall):** `zlib1g` 1.2.13 in the Debian 12 base of both platform images, with no fixed package; the advisory names zlib 1.3.1.2 to 1.3.2, so Trivy's match on 1.2.13 may be wrong. The overflow needs a program calling the system zlib's `gzwrite`/`gzprintf` on a non-blocking descriptor: Node.js uses its own bundled zlib, and the runtime runs no system tool that does (UID 10001, no capabilities, read-only root). Waived until 2026-12-07 (`infra/docker/trivyignore.yaml`) and reviewed with the other base waivers on 1 Dec.

### 3.111 Scheduled rule-set cut-over (`rules`; PR-LDC-34, R1-D-113)

- **E (a cut-over the approver did not see):** Control: `effectiveFrom` is part of the R4 request's payload and summary, so the checker approves the instant with the digest; execution reads it from the approved payload only.
- **T (back-dating a decision table):** Control: the cut-over is never before execution (`greatest(requested, now())`), and `effectiveFrom` must be now or later at submission, so no past decision or as-of read changes.
- **D (stacked or overlapping cut-overs):** Control: one scheduled cut-over per rule set (`IPB-RULE-003` until it passes); windows meet exactly at the cut-over, and the version in force is the one whose window covers the instant.

### 3.112 Keycloak base: two more `sqlite-libs` findings (waived 30 days; R1-D-115)

- **T and D (a crafted SQLite file or changeset):** CVE-2026-119374 (FTS5 secure-delete) and CVE-2026-119379 (session changeset apply) are in UBI 9 `sqlite-libs` of the Keycloak 26.8.1-2 image, with no fixed build. Keycloak keeps its state in PostgreSQL, opens no SQLite database, builds no FTS5 index and applies no changesets, so no input reaches either path. Waived until 2026-12-10 (`infra/docker/trivyignore.yaml`), with the two earlier `sqlite-libs` findings (R1-D-109).
- **Reassessed 12 Nov (R1-D-134):** the move to Keycloak 26.8.2-0 leaves both findings (and the four 20 Dec waivers) unchanged; the later 26.8.2 rebuilds (-1, -2) carry the same `sqlite-libs` build. The exposure analysis above stands.

### 3.113 The AV port and the ClamAV adapter (`contracts`, `connectors/av`; PR-LDC-41, R1-D-117)

- **T (malware reaching a reader; IPB-02-44):** the platform never defined the AV port the upload pipeline needs, so each product scanned on its own. `FileScanPort.scan(ref)` answers `CLEAN`, `INFECTED` or `ERROR`, and `scanDecision` is the one mapping: only `CLEAN` releases a file; `INFECTED` is `IPB-SEC-011`; `ERROR` and anything unknown keep it quarantined (`IPB-SEC-015`). The adapter never throws for a scan that ran and failed, so a caller cannot release a file by catching an exception.
- **T (a scan that did not really happen):** the adapter reads the object from STORAGE and checks its SHA-256 against the reference before scanning (`hash_mismatch` otherwise); a file over `maxBytes` (keep it at or below clamd's `StreamMaxLength`), clamd's size-limit reply, a timeout, an engine error, an unreachable or disabled engine, and a signature set older than 48 hours (IPB-02-44 wants daily updates) are all `ERROR`. Archives are scanned by clamd (the contract leg finds EICAR inside a ZIP).
- **I (file bytes on the wire):** clamd has no TLS, so the adapter connects only when the configured host resolves to loopback, RFC 1918, CGNAT or IPv6 unique-local space; anything else is `ERROR` and nothing is sent. The deployment runs clamd in the same cluster with a network policy that admits only the services that scan. Only the verdict, the threat name and the engine version come back; bytes are never logged. Residency IN, class ceiling REGULATED (KYC images are uploads too).
- **D (engine down or out of date):** uploads stay quarantined, never released, until a scan succeeds; `health()` is `degraded` when signatures are stale and reports their freshness in `detail` (engine version, build time, age in hours, the limit; R1-D-143), so monitoring alerts before uploads stall; the detail holds no file, tenant or address data. Off behind `int.av.clamav.enabled` (PLATFORM, R4) until a deployment runs clamd with daily `freshclam`. The test image (`clamav/clamav:1.5.4`, pinned by digest) is used from 16 Nov under PL2-D-32; the IPB-01 alternatives need an external account and stay unproven.

### 3.114 Cancelling an approval in the caller's transaction (`tenancy/0022`; PR-LDC-37, R1-D-120)

- **E (withdrawing someone else's request):** `cancelIn` applies `cancel`'s rules unchanged (the maker, ORG_OWNER or PLT_SECURITY_OFFICER; the request must be in the transaction's tenant, which RLS also enforces) and refuses agents. Nothing about approving changes. **R:** the cancellation is ledgered in the same transaction with its reason (digest in the ledger, text on the request); a rolled-back product write rolls back the cancellation, its ledger entry and the inbox closure together. **I:** the reason is CONFIDENTIAL, at most 500 characters, and never in the `ApprovalCancelled` event.

### 3.115 Business ids on ledger entries (`audit/0010`, `audit/0011`; PR-LDC-30, R1-D-125)

- **T (rewriting what an entry concerns):** the ids are inside the entry's hash when present (the trigger builds the canonical form; TypeScript verification matches it), so changing, adding or removing an id breaks the chain at that entry; the ledger stays append-only. Older entries hash exactly as before. **I:** ids only, by pattern (no spaces, at most 128 characters, 16 per entry), never personal data; checked in the sink and by a database check. `history` reads under the ledger's forced RLS, so a tenant sees only its own. **D:** the GIN index is partial and built concurrently, so neither the migration nor the index blocks ledger writes.

### 3.116 Workbook forms (`export` `toXlsx`; PR-LDC-31 writer, R1-D-128)

- **T (formula injection, a workbook that runs something):** validations are built from typed values only (literal list values without commas or quotes, numeric and date bounds, a named range defined in the same call); there is no custom-formula validation and no caller formula text, so the writer's rule that no cell is a formula holds, and text cells are still neutralised. Named ranges are refused if they look like a cell reference, and sheet names in references are always quoted. **T (relying on protection):** sheet protection takes no password and is documented as a UI guard, not a control: a product re-checks every value when a workbook comes back (the bounded reader, M84, has its own entry in wave 14).

### 3.117 The ACCOUNTING sink interface (`contracts`, `testing`; PR-FBX-46, R1-D-122)

- **T (a batch posted twice, or changed after approval):** `post` is idempotent on the journal id and the batch's canonical SHA-256; the same id with a different digest is refused (`IPB-PORT-004`) and an acknowledged batch is never replayed. **T (unbalanced or fractional books):** `assertBalancedBatch` runs before any call: integer paise up to 10^15, at least one debit and one credit, debits equal credits per voucher, voucher references unique in the batch; nothing is half-posted. **E:** agents are refused (`IPB-SEC-004`); a user or the product's service posts. **I:** amounts and narrations are CONFIDENTIAL. Posting records money already moved and moves none. No adapter yet: the TallyPrime adapter's local agent runs on the tenant's own network, outside the cluster, so its transport, authentication and replay window get their own entry with the wave 14 design before the wave 15 build.

### 3.118 The CALENDAR port interface (`contracts`, `testing`; PR-LDC-53, #126, R1-D-126)

- **S (a stolen authorisation code, a forged callback):** the code flow uses PKCE S256 and a state of at least 128 bits bound to the person's session; a state or verifier that does not match is `IPB-SEC-017`; the redirect URI must be the fixed callback path on https (http only on localhost), with no query, fragment or credentials, and providers compare it exactly. **E:** only the person connects and disconnects their own calendar; the product's service may write entries to it, an agent never writes or deletes. **I (case details in a shared calendar):** `calendarPayload` is the only view that reaches the provider: `busy` sends the time only, `title` adds the title, only `full` carries the description and location; the product picks the level per entry. Tokens never leave the adapter (stored with `fieldCryptoForTenant`, never logged or returned) and are revoked at the provider on disconnect; scopes are limited to calendar events (runbook `calendar-oauth-apps.md`). **T:** entries are upserted and deleted by the product's external id and the adapter touches only entries it wrote. **D:** a revoked or failed connection writes nothing (`IPB-PORT-003`) and shows `token_revoked` to the person. The Microsoft 365 and Google adapters (GLOBAL residency; DPA reference required, IPB-DEV-05) stay off and unproven until the owner registers the applications.

### 3.119 The product MFA floor (`contracts`, `connectors/identity`; PR-LDC-55, R1-D-135)

- **E (a product loosening IPB-02-02):** `mfaFloor` only adds roles; `assertMfaForRoles` applies the platform floor on top of whatever floor it is given, so a hand-made floor object cannot drop a platform role. **S (a weak second factor):** for SMS-excluded roles the session must name a non-SMS factor in `amr`; a token that only asserts `mfa` is refused, and `amr` is bounded (16 entries, 32 characters). **D (a user locked out of the step that satisfies the floor):** `assertStepUpReachable` refuses to boot when a callback, sign-in, MFA-enrolment, refresh or sign-out route is behind the floor. **I:** the step-up answer names only the required `acr_values` and whether SMS is excluded; no role list leaves the server. The `acr_values` are checked against RFC 6750's header grammar before they are written into `WWW-Authenticate`.

- **Realm half (PR-FBX-49, R1-D-182):** the Keycloak realm marks every floor role (product roles included) with the MFA marker and gives its holders the enrolment action on creation, sync and in `requireMfaEnrolment`; re-applying a realm only adds roles and markers. An SMS-excluded role always enrols TOTP or WebAuthn. Zitadel forces MFA for every user with TOTP and U2F as the only second factors; its `amr` values are kept (they were collapsed to `mfa`, which made every SMS-excluded Zitadel session fail closed), and its `otp` is trusted as non-SMS only because the applied login policy allows no SMS or email OTP, which `readSettings` reads back.

### 3.120 Ordered approval levels (`tenancy/0023`; PR-LDC-38, #125, R1-D-136)

- **E (a level that loosens maker-checker):** levels only add to the action's rule: level 1's roles must be among the action's checker roles and it needs at least the risk class's checkers; agent roles cannot be a level; a definition outside the limits throws before anything is stored. **E (one person deciding twice):** the decision table's key (request, checker) holds across levels, the maker is refused (`IPB-SEC-007`) and agents are refused (`IPB-SEC-004`). **T (deciding out of order):** a decision is recorded at the level open when it is made, checked against that level's roles, under the request's row lock; a rejection at any level ends the request. **R:** each decision is ledgered with its level. **D:** the delay window starts only when the last level approves, so a later level cannot shorten it.

### 3.121 The TallyPrime agent (built; PR-FBX-46, R1-D-137, R1-D-178)

Designed in wave 14 (R1-D-137), built in wave 15 (R1-D-178; plan `docs/phases/R1-tallyprime-plan.md`): `TallyAccountingSink`, `TallyAgentService` and `TallyAgentGateway` in `@infrabyx/connectors/accounting`, migration `connectors/0007`, the agent `apps/tally-agent` and its image `infra/docker/tally-agent/Dockerfile`. Tests: 20 PostgreSQL cases (`tally.int.test.ts`: the ACCOUNTING contract cases against the adapter with the agent in-process, under both TallyPrime REMOTEID behaviours, plus enrolment, the agent API's refusals, one job in flight, expiry, RLS and the triggers), 21 agent cases, 7 proof cases (`agent-request-jws.test.ts`, with a `jose` differential), the image's hardening checks.

- **S (a rogue agent pulling a tenant's journals):** an agent exists only after the R3 request `accounting.agent.enrol` is approved by a different person (an agent may not make it), and becomes active only by claiming a one-time code (`tac1.<tenant>.<agent>.<256-bit secret>`, 30 minutes, shown once, stored as SHA-256 only, compared in constant time; re-issuing replaces it; five wrong codes lock it; every refused claim is ledgered). The agent generates its own P-256 key; the platform stores the public JWK and its thumbprint only, set once (trigger). Every call carries a detached JWS on the C-22 profile with its own `typ` (`ipb-agent-request+jws`, so neither a regulated-message JWS nor a request proof passes as the other) over the tenant, the agent, the method, the path, the body's SHA-256 and a `seq` that must exceed the agent's last (stored in the same statement), within 300 s back and 60 s ahead. Calls run in the agent's own tenant as `SVC_TALLY_AGENT` under forced RLS; a `kid` naming another tenant finds no agent. Revocation (a tenant admin, or `PLT_SECURITY_OFFICER` under break-glass) and re-enrolment (which revokes the previous agent in the same transaction; one active agent per tenant) refuse the next call; `revoked` is final. Refusals answer a problem document that does not say which check failed.
- **T (duplicate, overwritten or altered vouchers):** `REMOTEID` is **not** a uniqueness guarantee in TallyPrime (a second create may duplicate or overwrite, depending on the company's overwrite setting; Tally's Import Data FAQ and a community guide, 18 Nov 2026, `[VERIFY]` with a sandbox company). So the agent looks every voucher's `REMOTEID` up before creating it, creates only when it is absent, and acknowledges only when exactly one voucher reads back with the same date, type, ledgers, sides and amounts (`remote_mismatch` or `remote_duplicate` otherwise; never altered or deleted; the agent sends only `ACTION="Create"`). A lost import answer is settled by the read-back. A changed batch under the same journal id never leaves the platform (`IPB-PORT-004`); the batch and digest are immutable and `ACKNOWLEDGED` is final (trigger); a result is accepted only from the agent holding the job, for its current attempt, once, and must name every voucher of the batch. Amounts move as integer paise and are converted by string arithmetic only.
- **I:** the agent listens on no port and keeps only the job in hand and its own state file (identity, key, last `seq`; mode 0600, refused if others can read it). TallyPrime's plain-HTTP port must be a loopback or private address; the platform URL must be `https:`. Data class at most CONFIDENTIAL (batches, ledger references and balances are CONFIDENTIAL columns), residency IN. TallyPrime's error text is never relayed: reasons are fixed codes with platform text.
- **D:** one job in flight per agent with a 10-minute lease; a job or result at most 2 MiB; 1,000 vouchers; postings unclaimed for `int.accounting.tally.job_expiry_days` (7) are rejected `agent_unreachable`; the long poll is 1 to 55 s; a trial balance is answered from a read under 10 minutes old (and newer than the tenant's last acknowledged posting); TallyPrime's answers are read through a strict XML reader (no DTD or entity, 8 MiB, depth 32, 200,000 elements). An empty poll writes only `last_seq` and `last_poll_at` as `svc_tally_poll`, which can update just those two columns (no ledger row, as a rate-limit hit, R1-D-123); every claim, result and refusal is ledgered. The port's health is `degraded` after 15 minutes without a poll and `down` after 24 hours.
- **E (the agent as a foothold):** it talks only to the product's API and to TallyPrime; it runs as UID 10001 on a read-only root with no capabilities, `/data` and `/tmp` its only writable paths, from the platform's pinned Node base image, with `contracts`, `crypto` and `zod` as its only dependencies (Trivy: no HIGH or CRITICAL, 18 Nov 2026). The switch `int.accounting.tally.enabled` is off by default (R4).
- **Residual:** the TallyPrime XML shapes (the export by `REMOTEID` through a TDL filter, the closing balances as of `SVTODATE`, the error wording mapped to reason codes, the amount signs) follow Tally's documentation as far as it could be read from the session (help.tallysolutions.com was not reachable; search snippets only) and stay `[VERIFY]` until a TallyPrime sandbox company exists (#128); the adapter stays `unproven` and off until then. A voucher entered by a person in TallyPrime with the same `REMOTEID` between the agent's lookup and its create is caught by the read-back as `remote_duplicate`, never silently. The key on the tenant's machine is protected by the machine (as TallyPrime's own data is); a stolen state file lets its holder act as the agent until it is revoked.

- **The agent's key at rest and its life (owner answer #136 (4), R1-D-184, `connectors/0008`):** on Windows the key is a DPAPI blob for the agent's own service account (`ProtectedData`, `CurrentUser` scope), through Windows PowerShell 5.1 with the key on standard input only (no command line, no script text, so neither process listings nor script-block logging see it); a copied state file is useless on another machine or account. Elsewhere, or when DPAPI fails its self-test, the key stays in the mode-0600 file and the enrolment record and view carry `key_unencrypted`. A key lives 90 days from enrolment: the view warns from day 80 and the gateway refuses the agent from day 90, which bounds the use of a stolen key that nobody revoked. The runbook (`docs/runbooks/tally-agent.md`) says to revoke at once if the machine is lost or compromised. `key_protection` is set once with the key (the guard trigger). **Notices and re-enrolment (owner answer #139 (1), R1-D-186, `connectors/0009`):** a per-tenant job (`sendKeyNotices`, run through `TenantSweep`) tells the tenant's active ORG_ADMIN and ORG_OWNER members on day 80 and again on day 85 (`accounting.agent_key_expiring`, mandatory, in-app and email; vars are the agent's id and name, the day and the expiry date, all INTERNAL), once each: `key_notice_day` only grows (guard trigger) and the notice, the column and the ledger entry share one transaction. From day 90 the 403 carries a `detail` with the re-enrolment steps; only an agent whose proof verified receives it, so it tells a forger nothing. The agent takes a new one-time code from its state directory without a restart (a refused code is removed), so a rotation needs no access to the service beyond the state directory.

### 3.122 Projection rule sets (`rules/0004`; PR-LDC-49, R1-D-139)

- **R (decisions without a record):** `project` writes nothing, so it is confined to rule sets created for projection; the purpose is fixed at creation and `evaluate` refuses a projection set, `project` a decision-grade one (`IPB-RULE-003`). A projection result has no decision id, so it cannot be cited as a decision. Publishing a projection set stays an R4 request. **D:** at most 1,000 inputs a call, each under the engine's evaluation timeout. **I:** results return only to the caller in its tenant (RLS on the rule set).

### 3.123 Inbound mail replies (built; PR-LDC-50, R1-D-141, R1-D-177)

- **As built** (plan `docs/phases/R1-inbound-mail-plan.md`; `notify/0007`, `NotifyService.receiveReplies`, `SesEmail.verifyInbound`). Tests: `notify` `reply.test.ts` (17) and `reply.int.test.ts` (12, PostgreSQL), `connectors` `ses-inbound.test.ts` (14, with the shared inbound contract) and the EMAIL contract leg (S3 action on moto).
- **S (a spoofed, forwarded or replayed reply):** a reply counts only with a stored, unexpired token under its use limit (50), found by its SHA-256, in a tenant with `ntf.reply.enabled` on and the token's domain equal to `ntf.reply.domain`; the token's recipient as the sole `From` (address hash, C-23); and DMARC `pass`, or no enforceable DMARC policy with an aligned DKIM pass and a strictly aligned SPF pass. The verdicts come only from the SNS-signed SES receipt (C-13, a topic separate from delivery events); a forged `Authentication-Results` header in the message is ignored (tested). An SNS redelivery is a duplicate (unique per tenant, adapter and vendor message id) and consumes nothing. **T (parser attacks):** 10 MiB, 998-octet lines and a charset allow-list checked on the bytes before parsing; postal-mime 4.2.1 (MIT-0, no dependencies) with 200 parts, depth 10 and 64 KiB of headers; a forwarded message is an attachment, never parsed inline; HTML is converted to text and never rendered; control characters removed; text capped at 20 000 characters. Accepted replies are immutable (trigger; no update grant). **R:** one ledger entry per minted token, per accepted reply (token hash prefix, sender hash, attachment digests and states) and per first drop of a day per sender and reason; the cross-tenant token lookup by `svc_notify_reply` (granted `token_hash, tenant_id` only) is ledgered per tenant by `ServiceReader`. **I:** tokens stored as SHA-256 only; no address stored (hashes only); dropped bodies never stored or ledgered; the `NotificationReplyReceived` event carries ids and a count, never text; `notify.reply` is tenant-scoped under forced RLS. **E (malware):** every attachment is written to `tenant/<tenant>/notify/<ULID>.bin` as `application/octet-stream`, scanned, and `RELEASED` only on `CLEAN` (§3.113); the declared type and file name are kept as data, never used as a path. **D:** per-token use limit, 20 attachments, the size bounds, drops counted by reason (`onDrop`); replies are off by default. **Residual:** SES receiving is the only inbound adapter (Brevo's inbound parsing carries neither a signature nor a receiving-side verdict, owner question #135); a sender domain with `p=none` whose mail is relayed through a third party (envelope-from on another domain) cannot reply until it aligns SPF or publishes an enforcing DMARC policy. Attachments are stored and scanned before the accepting transaction, so a reply that loses the use-limit race (or a concurrent redelivery) leaves its scanned objects unreferenced in the tenant's storage; they are never served (no record points at them) and go with the tenant's storage lifecycle.

### 3.124 The shared rate limiter (`tenancy/0024`; PR-LDC-54, #129, R1-D-149)

- **I (what a counter reveals):** a row holds the tenant, the product's bucket name, the window and a count. The key (a public token, an address, a client id) is never stored; the row has HMAC-SHA-256 of the bucket and the key under the tenant's rate-limit sub-key of the product's blind-index root (PL1-C-05, R1-D-37; no new cryptography). Two tenants' hashes of one key differ, and a database reader cannot test a guessed token without the root key. **E (a write path without the ledger):** a hit writes no ledger row (R1-D-123), so it runs as `svc_rate_limit`, which may only select, insert and update the counter table under the tenant's RLS: it cannot delete, cannot read any other table, and is reachable from `app_rw` only, never from `app_ro` or an auditor role (the read-only reachability test covers it). **T (erasing evidence through the sweep):** the one deletion the owner allowed (#129) is enforced by the database: a restrictive policy admits a delete only where `window_end <= now()`, so even `app_rw` cannot delete a live window, and nothing else is deletable. Each sweep run writes one ledger entry with the count and the range of window ends. **D:** hits past the limit are capped at the limit plus one, so a flood updates one row and never grows it; windows are 1 second to 1 day; the limiter fails closed (an error, never "allowed") when the database is down. Rotating the blind-index root resets every counter, which only lets a client start a fresh window once.

### 3.125 Web snapshots as evidence (`connectors` `egress/governed`, `web-snapshot`, `connectors/0006`; PR-LDC-40, R1-D-117, R1-D-150, R1-D-176)

Designed in wave 14 (R1-D-150), built in wave 15 (R1-D-176; plan `docs/phases/R1-web-snapshot-plan.md`).

- **S (server-side request forgery):** the fetch goes through `EgressPort` only, the `governed` adapter. Each hop must be `https:` on port 443 with no user-info, a host name (an IP literal is refused) on the tenant's `int.web_snapshot.allow_hosts`. Every address the name resolves to must be public: IPv4 outside every IANA special-purpose block (RFC 6890; loopback, private, CGNAT, link-local and its metadata address, documentation, benchmarking, multicast, reserved), IPv6 inside global unicast `2000::/3` and outside `2001::/23`, `2001:db8::/32`, `2002::/16` and `3fff::/20` (so ULA, link-local, NAT64, IPv4-mapped and multicast are out). One refused address refuses the hop (a mixed DNS answer is how rebinding is staged). The socket connects to the address that was checked (the request's `lookup` returns only it), and TLS still verifies the certificate against the host name. At most 5 redirects, each hop checked and resolved again; a redirect to http, to another port, off the list or to a refused address is refused.
- **T (a copy that is not what was served):** the stored object is the body as received (decoded from gzip, deflate or br; the encoding recorded), with the final URL, the redirect chain, status (200 only), media type, `fetched_at` and SHA-256, in `connectors.web_snapshot` with the `connectors.web_snapshot.archive` ledger entry in the same transaction. A trigger keeps every fetched field immutable; only a quarantined row's scan may move it to `RELEASED` or `REJECTED`; there is no delete grant. `open` refuses bytes that no longer hash to the record (`IPB-SEC-015`, `hash_mismatch`).
- **E (active content):** no script runs: nothing is rendered and no sub-resource is fetched. The media type must be HTML, XHTML, plain text, PDF, PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP (SVG is refused), and a binary type must start with its signature, so a page cannot pass as a PDF. The copy is scanned by the AV port (§3.113) and released only on `CLEAN`; `ERROR` keeps it quarantined until `rescan`, `INFECTED` rejects it. It is served only through `webSnapshotDownloadHeaders`: `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'`, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`.
- **D:** 10 MB and 20 seconds by default (25 MB and 60 seconds at most), one deadline for the whole chain including DNS; a `Content-Length` over the cap is refused before reading, the body is read capped, and decoding is capped too, so a decompression bomb stops at the cap. Each tenant has `int.web_snapshot.per_hour` (60) snapshots an hour through the shared rate limiter (§3.124).
- **I:** only the public URL is sent, with no cookies (none sent, none kept across hops), no `Authorization` or `Referer`, and a fixed User-Agent (`InfrabyxWebSnapshot/1.0`) naming no tenant; the tenant id never leaves in the request. The URLs are CONFIDENTIAL in the table (they show what a tenant is watching); the ledger entry names hosts and keeps the full URLs under its digest.
- **E (who may fetch):** a tenant user or service in its own tenant only. Agents (`IPB-SEC-004`), impersonation (`IPB-SEC-003`), external principals, break-glass, platform contexts and platform staff (`IPB-SEC-004`) are refused. Both switches are off by default (`int.egress.governed.enabled`, `int.web_snapshot.enabled`, R4), and an empty allow-list fetches nothing.
- **Residual:** the address ranges are from RFC 6890 and the IANA registries as known on 18 Nov 2026; www.iana.org was not reachable from the build session, so they are marked `[VERIFY]` in the code for the next review. The guard runs in the product's process; the deployment's network policy (or cloud NAT with egress rules, the IPB-01 alternative) should still deny the pod private ranges. A page can differ by client (a server may answer the platform's User-Agent differently from a browser), so a snapshot is evidence of what the platform was served. An object written before a failed database write is left unreferenced in the tenant's STORAGE (no record points at it).

### 3.126 Letterhead text, cover and subdomain on the tenant brand layer (PR-LDC-43, R1-D-153)

- **T (markup or script through the letterhead):** letterhead text is plain text: controls, bidirectional marks, `<`, `>` and character references are refused, and so is any text with a `javascript:`, `vbscript:`, `data:`, `file:` or `blob:` scheme, so even a renderer that auto-links has nothing to run. Renderers still escape it. The cover is a tenant-storage asset checked by content as the letterhead is (sanitised SVG only). **S (impersonating another tenant or the platform by subdomain):** a label is one lower-case host label. Service, product and channel names are reserved, `xn--` labels (homographs) are refused, and the host is unique group-wide across pack hosts and tenant domains (code and database triggers). A tenant learns nothing about other tenants' labels except that one is taken. **E:** every field goes through the layer's R3 maker-checker with the ledger entry in the same transaction; nothing changes DNS or certificates (the product's deployment).

### 3.127 Reading uploaded workbooks (`export` `readXlsx`; PR-LDC-31 reader, R1-D-154)

- **D (decompression bombs):** sizes, counts and ratios come from the central directory and are checked before any inflate. Every inflate is capped at the part's declared size, so a part that lies about its size is refused, never inflated further, and the total across parts is capped as well. **T/I (XML attacks):** no DTD is processed: a DOCTYPE or entity declaration is refused, and only the five predefined entities and character references are decoded, so there is no billion-laughs expansion and no external entity fetch. External relationships (links to other files or URLs) are never followed. **E (active content):** macro and ActiveX projects are refused, and a formula is never returned or evaluated, only its cached value. A value a product writes back out goes through `toXlsx`'s formula neutralising. **T (path traversal):** entry names with `..`, a leading `/`, a backslash or a NUL are refused, and nothing is written to disk. The AV scan (§3.113) still runs before the reader.

### 3.128 Storage compartments (`contracts` STORAGE, `connectors/storage`; PR-LDC-60, R1-D-158)

- **I (a board paper readable with the organisation's general key):** an object in a compartment (`BOARD_CONFIDENTIAL`, `SEALED`) is written only to that compartment's bucket under that compartment's SSE-KMS key. The adapter refuses to start when a compartment bucket or key equals any other bucket or key, and refuses a compartment write with no bucket or key configured; it never falls back to the class bucket or key. A key holder for general objects therefore cannot decrypt compartment objects, and the key policy of a compartment key can be limited to the roles that need it. **I (a tenant with its own CMK):** such a tenant must supply a separate key per compartment. A tenant key function that returns its general key, a missing key or a key shared with another compartment is refused at write, so an older single-key configuration fails closed. **T (moving an object between compartments):** a write or upload URL for an existing key in another class or compartment is refused, and an upload URL signs the compartment's key header, so the uploader cannot choose another key. **R:** the compartment is part of the object's location, read back on `get`; what the product records about access stays in its ledger. **Infrastructure (wave 15, R1-D-170):** the OpenTofu `platform` modules take `storage_compartments` (default `[]`) and create, per location, a bucket that copies its class's controls (AWS: Object Lock compliance with the class's retention, a locked DR copy replicated under the compartment key's DR replica, private, versioned, TLS-only; Azure: GZRS with the class's immutability policy and lock switch, Entra ID only, no shared keys; GCP: dual-region with a locked retention policy, uniform access, public access prevention) under a key of its own of the platform key's kind (AWS multi-region KMS key with a DR replica; Azure RSA-HSM 3072 in the primary vault; GCP HSM key in the multi-region ring); a precondition refuses a location without its own key or with a key equal to the platform key or another location's, and the `tofu test` of each cloud proves it. Only the existing principals reach the keys (AWS: the S3 replication role decrypts with the primary and encrypts with the DR replica; Azure: the vault-scoped CMK identity; GCP: the storage service agent). **Residual:** the AWS keys use the account's default key policy, as the platform key does; a product that wants a compartment key usable only by some workloads narrows its key policy and grants in its environment stack. The modules are tested against mock providers, never applied from here.

### 3.129 Text from uploaded PDFs (`contracts` PDF, `connectors/pdf`; PR-LDC-39, R1-D-160)

- **T (an unscanned or swapped file parsed):** `extractText` takes the AV port's result with the file and refuses before reading a byte unless the verdict is `CLEAN` and the scan's SHA-256 is the file's (`assertScannedClean`: `INFECTED` is `IPB-SEC-011`, anything else `IPB-SEC-015`); the bytes read from STORAGE must hash to it too, so a file replaced after its scan stays quarantined. **D (decompression bombs, deep nesting, huge content):** FlateDecode is the only filter inflated, each inflate capped at the smaller of the stream limit (16 MiB) and 200 times its compressed size, and all inflates together at 128 MiB; file size (25 MiB), pages (500, counted in the tree whatever `/Count` says), objects (100,000), and nesting of arrays, dictionaries, the page tree and form XObjects (32) are limited. A caller may lower a limit; a higher one is capped at the platform maximum. **D/E (a hostile file hanging or exhausting the product's process):** the parse runs in a `worker_thread` with its own V8 heap (256 MiB, at most 512), a 4 MiB stack, an empty environment and no inherited flags, and is terminated at its deadline (30 s, at most 120 s); out of time or memory is `IPB-SEC-013`. **E (active content):** only the catalog's page tree, page contents, fonts, ToUnicode maps and form XObjects are read; actions (JavaScript, Launch), the name tree, embedded files and annotations are never resolved, so nothing is executed, opened or fetched, and the parser has no network or file access. Encrypted files are refused (`IPB-SEC-010`), never decrypted. **I:** refusal details describe the structure, never the content; the text goes back to the caller only and is not logged. **Residual:** a file's ToUnicode map can make the extracted text differ from what the page shows, so extracted text is evidence of the text layer, not of what a reader saw; text follows content-stream order, so a generator that draws a line out of order gives that order; ArrayBuffers (the file and inflated streams) sit outside the V8 heap limit and are bounded by the size and inflate limits instead; the parser is the platform's own code, covered by hand-built hostile fixtures and not yet fuzzed.

### 3.130 Entitled reveal of a CONFIDENTIAL input in `explain` (`rules`; PR-LDC-59, #130, R1-D-157 (1), R1-D-173)

- **I (a value shown to the wrong person):** `explain` stays name-only above INTERNAL unless the caller holds a grant from `entitleReveal`. The platform refuses before it asks the product's `revealRule` when the input is not CONFIDENTIAL (REGULATED is never revealed; SECRET never enters rules), is not declared in the decision's version, or was not recorded; the decision is read under RLS, so another tenant's decision is not found. The grant names one input of one decision, for the principal and tenant that asked, and is used once within 60 seconds; the value goes back to that caller only, and nothing is written back (no decision-log update, no outbox event). The other sealed inputs stay name-only, although the sealed envelope (sealed at the highest class among them) is opened in memory to take the one value. **E (an agent, platform staff, support or a break-glass holder reading tenant inputs):** agents, platform contexts, any `PLT_*` role, break-glass sessions, impersonations and external principals are refused before the product's rule runs, and again when the grant is used. **T (forging or replaying a grant):** a grant is a frozen object recorded in the issuing service's private `WeakMap`; a copy, a JSON round trip, a look-alike or a grant from another instance is not in the map and is refused; first use removes it, so a replay is refused. No token, signature or key is involved. **R:** every reveal writes `rules.decision.input_revealed` in the same transaction (actor, decision, input, rule id and grant id as business ids, the purpose under the entry's digest, never the value); if the ledger write fails, nothing is returned. **Residual:** the product's `revealRule` is the product's own policy, and the platform cannot judge whether it is right; the purpose text is under the digest, so a product that needs it readable keeps it in its own record of the request; refused attempts are not ledgered by the platform (the product may log its own refusals).

- **R (repudiation and probing; owner answer #132 (4), (5); R1-D-187; `audit/0012`):** every reveal names a purpose code from the product's frozen list, kept as the business id `purpose:<code>` beside the purpose digest. Every refusal, at either step, is kept in `audit.security_event` (append-only for every role, by trigger and grants; forced RLS; written in its own short transaction as `svc_security_event`, which may only insert), so a refused attempt is on record although the attempt rolled back; the row holds the principal, the decision id, the input name, the reason, the purpose code and the rule id only after each passed a strict pattern, so a probe cannot plant a value or free text in it (a malformed name is left out, not stored). Refusals are counted per principal with the shared rate limiter (hashed key, no ledger row per hit); the threshold-th refusal within the window (5 in 15 minutes by default, settings `rules.reveal.refusal_alert_threshold` and `_window_minutes`, PLT_SECURITY_OFFICER, R4) publishes one `RevealRefusalsRepeated` per window through the outbox, ledgered by the service principal `svc_rules_security`, for the product's security on-call. A `revealRule` without the sink, the counter and the outbox refuses to start. **Residual:** the security events have no export yet (IPB-02-54's SIEM stream is a later piece); they are kept like the ledger until the owner's retention schedule says otherwise; a sink or counter failure fails the refused call loudly (a 500, not a silent refusal).

### 3.131 Dated, cited and locked rule parameters (`rules/0005`; PR-LDC-57, R1-D-146, R1-D-175)

- **T (a tenant or one person changing a statutory value):** a parameter schedule is written only in a platform context in `tnt_platform` (the service refuses with `IPB-UX-011`; forced RLS refuses a direct insert or update from any tenant context, and a `check` pins `tenant_id` to `tnt_platform`). Publication is the R4 request `rules.ruleset.publish` (platform checkers, maker ≠ checker, the R4 delay, the scheduled cut-over); the executor re-checks the platform context, the version's state, its approval id and its digest. A published version's body, digest and cut-over, and a retired version's end, are immutable (trigger); a correction is a new version with a required reason, and the type is fixed for the schedule's life. A dated parameter is locked: no setting is read for it and it has no default, so a tenant cannot override it. **R:** each decision records, per dated parameter, the value, the schedule key and version, the entry's date and its citation, in the same transaction as its ledger entry; every schedule write is ledgered (`rules.schedule.*`). **I:** none: the values and citations are public statutory text (`body` is PUBLIC); no tenant data enters a schedule. **D (a missing or out-of-bounds value):** no entry in force gives `UNDETERMINED`/`parameter_not_in_force` and an entry outside the rule's bounds gives `parameter_invalid`, never a guess; both reach the human queue. A rule set cannot be drafted against an unknown, unpublished or mistyped schedule. **Residual:** a wrong value approved by two platform people is applied until corrected (the citation and the ledger show who and when); the entry date is read as an India-time calendar day (R1-D-175 (1)), so a product in another zone must pass `asOf` at its own midnight.

### 3.132 Runtime fakes without the test runner (`@infrabyx/fakes`; PR-FBX-48, R1-D-181, ADR-0011)

- **Supply chain (a test toolchain in a production tree):** the port fakes moved to `@infrabyx/fakes`, whose runtime dependencies are `contracts`, `crypto` and `config` only, with no peers; a package test fails on any import of a test runner, container library, database driver or `@infrabyx/testing`. A product that wires fakes in a fixture environment no longer carries `vitest`, `vite`, `picomatch`, Testcontainers (`ssh2`, `docker-modem`) or `pg` in its production audit or image; the reference API's production deploy dropped from 635 to 422 packages, and the R1-D-174 `picomatch` waiver is gone.
- **E (a fake reaching production):** unchanged: a fake is never an adapter registered for `staging`, `pre-prod`, `prod` or `dr`; products keep failing closed above staging. Moving the fakes changes no behaviour: `@infrabyx/testing` re-exports the same objects (a test checks identity), and the contract cases still run against them.

### 3.133 Reads joined to an ambient mutation (`PgKernel.ambient` `joinReads`; PR-UDF-037, R1-D-188)

- **E (a read that writes, or runs with the app role):** a joined read runs on the ambient connection in a savepoint as the kernel's read role with `transaction_read_only` on; the savepoint is always rolled back, so a write is refused by PostgreSQL (`25006`) and nothing it did survives; the role, read-only flag and `app.*` settings come back with the rollback. Tested: the read's `current_user` is the read role, a table only the app role may read is refused, the ambient transaction is read-write as the app role afterwards.
- **I (a read seeing another tenant's or principal's uncommitted rows):** only a read with the ambient call's tenant, principal, scope and break-glass grant joins; support, external and platform service reads never join (their gates and roles stay); RLS applies inside the savepoint as in any transaction.
- **T (interleaved statements on one connection):** joined reads take the same one-at-a-time stack and in-flight wait as joined mutations (R1-D-92, §3.94); a concurrent sibling is refused (queued since §3.161), and a read still running when the ambient callback ends fails the transaction. Opt-in (`joinReads`, default false), so no running product changes behaviour.

### 3.134 Refusal label and retry wait in problem responses (`contracts` `toProblem`; PR-UDF-038, R1-D-189)

- **I (meta leaking through the problem):** only `meta.reason` matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,63}$` (a label, never a value: an e-mail, a sentence or a 12-digit number does not match) and, on a 429 or 503, an integer wait from 1 to 86400 leave the platform; every other `meta` key stays inside, as before. Tested with value-like inputs.

### 3.135 OpenJDK Base64 intrinsic, CVE-2026-114938 (Keycloak customer image, Compose, test harness; owner answer #143, OWNER-WAIVER-CRIT-01, R1-D-192)

- **E (remote code execution before authentication):** HotSpot's Base64 intrinsic corrupts memory on x86-64 CPUs with AVX-512 VBMI (unpatched OpenJDK 17.0.6 and later). Keycloak decodes attacker-supplied Base64 before authentication (Red Hat RHSB-2026-018), so the identity provider is reachable from the internet through the ingress. The pinned base's `java-21-openjdk-headless` 1:21.0.13.0.9-1.2.el9 has no fixed build. **Mitigation:** the intrinsic is off on every node whatever its CPU: the customer image's entrypoint (`ipb-kc.sh`) appends `-XX:-UseBASE64Intrinsics` last to `JAVA_OPTS_APPEND` before `kc.sh`, so a deployment's own JVM options cannot drop it; Compose and the test harness set it for stock Keycloak, Gotenberg's pdftk JVM (`JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS`) and OpenSearch (`OPENSEARCH_JAVA_OPTS`). The other shipped images carry no JVM (scan of 19 Nov 2026; `infra/docker/jvm-images.mjs`; a new image fails the static test until it is classified).
- **T (the flag turned back on):** `_JAVA_OPTIONS` is applied after the command line, and `JDK_JAVA_OPTIONS` and `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS` are read outside `kc.sh`; the entrypoint refuses to start (exit 64) when any of them names `UseBASE64Intrinsics`. A deployment that replaces the entrypoint loses this; the README and guide §7cp say to keep it or to set the flag and check the live JVM.
- **Assurance:** the image build fails unless its JVM resolves the flag to `false {command line}`; the `containers` job reads it from the live JVMs with `jcmd VM.flags` (Keycloak, OpenSearch) or the JVM's own flag dump (Gotenberg's pdftk during a real merge, LocalStack), including hostile `JAVA_OPTS` and `JAVA_OPTS_APPEND`. The CRITICAL waiver is scoped by purl to that one OpenJDK build, lives in the same required job, and ends on the earlier of a fixed image and 15 Dec 2026.
- **Four HIGH findings in the same OpenJDK build (R1-D-208, waived to 15 Dec under the standing rule):** CVE-2026-114939 (JGSS cross-realm tickets: the advisory limits it to sandboxed untrusted code; no realm configures Kerberos or SPNEGO); CVE-2026-114940 (JSSE TLS client: Keycloak is a client only to configured brokers and NOTIFY, never to a request-supplied address); CVE-2026-114941 (HotSpot compiler integer compression: high complexity, mainly a crash; no documented JVM flag; a crashed node restarts while the others serve); CVE-2026-114942 (vector masks: Keycloak does not load `jdk.incubator.vector`). Scoped by purl to the pinned build; a fixed Keycloak image clears them with CVE-2026-114938.
- **Residual risk:** the intrinsic off costs Base64 throughput only (the pure-Java path is used); the vulnerable code remains in the image until the fixed build. Products that run another JVM image, or override the entrypoint, carry their own decision (guide §7cp (4)).

### 3.136 DigiLocker start and keyed pulls (`connectors/0010` `connectors.kyc_digilocker_pull`, `KycPort.startDigilocker`; PR-UDF-040, R1-D-193)

- **S (the customer sent back to a page the product does not own):** `startDigilocker` refuses a return address that is not `https:`, carries a user name or password or a 12-digit run, or whose host is not exactly one of the tenant's allowed hosts (an adapter option, empty by default, so every start is refused until the product sets it), with `meta.reason: "return_url_not_allowed"` and before any vendor call. The sign-in address is built on Digio's gateway host constant, not taken from the reply [VERIFY the address form against Digio's sandbox]. Tested in the contract cases (http, another host, a look-alike suffix, user information) and the unit tests.
- **I (the key, the session or a document leaking through the new store):** a row holds the idempotency key and the consent session as SHA-256 only (each with its own prefix), the request's hash, a state and, once done, each document's STORAGE reference, issuer, evidence and signature result; never a document's content. Forced RLS by tenant; the ledger entry of each step names the row id (or the key's hash) and the action only. Tested: another tenant reads nothing and cannot write a row for the first, the key never appears in the table or the ledger, no storage key or session in the ledger.
- **T (a retry served another request's documents, or a session used twice):** a record is found by tenant, adapter and key hash, and its request hash covers the session and the sorted document types: another session or other types under the same key is `IPB-PORT-004` (`idempotency_conflict`). One record per session per tenant and adapter (a unique constraint), and every pull, keyed or not, still claims the session once in the replay store (400 days), so a second key on a used session is `consent_used`. A record moves once, from pending to done or refused (a trigger). A pull that reserved its key and died is finished by a retry with the key, which then takes the session's claim as its own; between the crash and that retry an unkeyed pull of the same session in the same tenant could also succeed (narrow, the same tenant's own call, accepted).
- **D / retention:** a record lives 24 hours (IPB-10-07's window; #144); an expired record is deleted when its key or session comes again, or by `purgeExpired` (ledgered). An interrupted pull can leave a copy of a document in the tenant's REGULATED STORAGE with no reference to it, as before this change; a retry writes new objects rather than overwriting.

### 3.137 Aadhaar OTP e-KYC for a notified RE (`connectors/0011` `connectors.kyc_aadhaar_otp_verify`, `KycPort.aadhaarOtpRequest` and `aadhaarOtpVerify`; PR-UDF-041, R1-D-194)
