- **E (an RE that may not use Aadhaar OTP e-KYC using it):** only an RE notified under PML Act s.11A may (KYC Directions 2025 para 23 proviso (i) [VERIFY the paragraph]). Every adapter and the fake refuse both calls `not_configured`, as a definite refusal, unless built with `notifiedEntity: true`; the check runs first, before the adapter flag, the parse or any vendor call, so a refusal sends nothing and reads nothing. The router does not pass `not_configured` on to the next adapter. Tested in the contract cases (a counted vendor call) and the adapter tests.
- **I (the Aadhaar number or VID leaking):** `uid` is REGULATED, checked locally (12 digits, the first 2 to 9, Verhoeff; or a 16-digit Verhoeff VID; grouped forms refused) and refused `uid_invalid` before any vendor call; it goes in the one request body to the KUA and nowhere else: not in the URL, an answer, an error (fixed details; Zod errors carry no input), a log, a ledger entry or a stored row, and the adapter drops it when the call returns. Answers are checked for a 12- or 16-digit run before they leave. The e-KYC response is written to the RE's REGULATED STORAGE unchanged, unless it carries a number that reads as an Aadhaar number or VID: then it is refused `ekyc_holds_number` and not stored, because that number belongs only in the RE's Aadhaar Data Vault ([VERIFY] whether the KUA's response carries it; #145). The contract sweeps every answer, error and, with the PostgreSQL store, the ledger and the table.
- **I (the code leaking through the new store):** a row holds the idempotency key as SHA-256 and the request as an HMAC-SHA-256 over the transaction and the code, keyed by the idempotency key and the transaction together; no row holds either in clear, so the million six-digit codes cannot be tried against a row (#145). The result kept is the e-KYC's STORAGE reference, the evidence, the signature result and the last four digits (CONFIDENTIAL). Forced RLS by tenant; the ledger names the row, the key's hash, the state and the refusal label only.
- **T (a retry served another request's answer):** a record is found by tenant, adapter and key hash; another code or transaction under the key is `IPB-PORT-004` (`idempotency_conflict`). The first answer is kept (a result, or the vendor's `otp_invalid` or `otp_expired`) and returned to a retry without a vendor call; another key on a used transaction goes to the vendor, which refuses it. A record moves once, from pending to done or refused (a trigger). A vendor outage leaves the key pending, so its retry goes to the vendor again.
- **D / retention:** a record lives 24 hours (IPB-10-07's window, as #144), deleted on reuse or by `purgeExpired` (ledgered). A verify that stored the e-KYC and died before recording it leaves an unreferenced object in the tenant's REGULATED STORAGE; its retry is refused `otp_expired` (the transaction is spent). How long the RE keeps the e-KYC is the product's retention rule, not the platform's.

### 3.138 The shared no-Aadhaar rule, its scanner and the test-database sweep (`@infrabyx/contracts/aadhaar`, `infrabyx-aadhaar-scan`, `startPostgres({ beforeDrop })`; PR-UDF-042, R1-D-195)

- **I (an Aadhaar number or VID reaching a fixture, a test database, a log or a model prompt):** one rule, `findAadhaarLike` (12 digits with the first 2 to 9 and Verhoeff, or a 16-digit Verhoeff VID, plain or grouped in fours), is what the AI scrubber tokenises (IPB-03-27), what the KYC adapters refuse to store (§3.137) and what the platform's CI sweeps for: the fixtures, recorded vendor stubs and golden sets on every PR (`pnpm aadhaar:scan`), and the NN integration suite's database before it is dropped. A find fails the step; it is never allow-listed.
- **I (the finds themselves):** a find carries the kind, offsets and the last four digits only (`XXXX XXXX dddd`); the CLI prints file, line and that; the database sweep names schema, table, column and `ctid`. Neither prints, logs or stores more of the number; the scrubber slices the text it already holds.
- **E (the sweep's reach):** `scanDatabaseForAadhaar` is given a superuser client, which row-level security does not bind, so it reads every tenant's rows; it lives in `@infrabyx/testing` (a devDependency) and is meant for test databases only. The CLI reads only the paths it is given, follows no symbolic link and makes no network call.
- **T / D (what it does not see):** a number split by two or more characters (`\r\n`, `-`), digits other than ASCII, digits inside a run of letters for the sweep (which joins letters, so hashes and base64 do not fail it), UUID-shaped text, binary files and files over 5 MiB (named, not read), and every path outside the scan list (unit-test sources hold synthetic numbers). The masked form `XXXX XXXX dddd` and the keyword rule stay with the scrubber. #146 records these defaults.
- **R / availability:** if a `beforeDrop` hook throws, `end()` still drops the database and stops the container before it rejects, so a failed check leaves no database holding test data behind.

### 3.139 Bank account checks by mode and by key (`connectors/0012` `connectors.kyc_bank_account_verify`, `KycPort.bankAccountVerify`, `KYC_ADAPTER_FUNDING`; PR-UDF-043, R1-D-196)

- **T (money moved without approval, or twice):** a penny drop credits the customer's account, so the platform's Digio and IDfy adapters do not make one: they refuse `penny_drop` `IPB-PORT-002` `mode_unsupported` right after the parse, before the flag, the store or any vendor call, and their request bodies carry the pennyless mode only (`PENNY_LESS`, `nf_verification`). No silent fallback to another mode. For any adapter that does make penny drops, the port's contract is one vendor call per idempotency key: a keyed retry gets the first answer from the store, a penny-drop key left pending is refused `pending_unresolved` and never re-sent (P-07), and `kycMethodRouter` does not move a keyed check or a penny drop to the next adapter when one is unavailable (it may have reached its vendor). Tested in the contract cases (a counted vendor call) and the adapter tests.
- **R (who funds the credit):** `KYC_ADAPTER_FUNDING` states, per adapter, whether the provider or the tenant funds a penny drop, or that it is not yet known, with the source, so an LSP agreement can record that no LSP account is in the flow of funds (CF para 10(1)-(3)). Digio and IDfy are `unknown` [VERIFY] until each provider's documentation and the RE's agreement confirm it (#147). A statement only: the platform holds no account and moves no money.
- **I (the account number leaking through the new store):** a row holds the idempotency key as SHA-256 and the request (IFSC, account number, name, mode) as an HMAC-SHA-256 under a key derived from the idempotency key; neither the account number nor the IFSC is kept in clear, and no unkeyed hash of them is kept, as an account number is short enough to guess (#147). The result kept is the port's answer (status, mode, the name at the bank and its match, the evidence; REGULATED). Forced RLS by tenant; the ledger names the row, the key's hash, the mode and the state only. Answers and errors carry no account number and no Aadhaar-like number (the contract sweeps them with `findAadhaarLike`); a vendor's account name carrying six or more digits is refused.
- **T (a retry served another request's answer):** a record is found by tenant, adapter and key hash; another account, IFSC, name or mode under the key is `IPB-PORT-004` (`idempotency_conflict`), checked before any vendor call. A record moves once, from pending to done (a trigger); the mode cannot change.
- **D (a refused mode as an outage):** `mode_unsupported` uses `IPB-PORT-002`, the code the core router counts as unavailable; `tripsBreaker` does not count it, so a product asking for a mode an adapter lacks cannot open the KYC circuit. **Retention:** a record lives 24 hours (IPB-10-07's window, as #144), deleted on reuse or by `purgeExpired` (ledgered).

### 3.140 Liveness and face match by mode and by key (`connectors/0013` `connectors.kyc_score_check`, `KycPort.livenessCheck`, `KycPort.faceMatch`, `KYC_ADAPTER_RETENTION`; PR-UDF-044, R1-D-197)

- **S (a blink check passed off as another mode):** a product asks for `passive`, `active_gesture` or `active_voice`; an adapter that cannot honour it refuses `IPB-PORT-002` `mode_unsupported` right after the parse, before the flag, the store or any vendor call, and never falls back to another mode, and the answer names the mode used. IDfy's photo liveness is passive, so the adapter honours `passive` only [VERIFY] (#148); Digio has no liveness path. Tested in the contract cases (a counted vendor call) and the adapter tests.
- **T (a score read wrongly):** a score is a finite number from 0 to 1, and the answer names the threshold applied (`passed` is `score >= threshold`). An adapter reads a vendor score on its known scale (IDfy: `IDFY_SCORE_SCALE`, 1 [VERIFY]) and refuses one off it `IPB-PORT-003` `score_out_of_range`; it no longer guesses a 0-to-100 scale from a value above 1 and no longer clamps, which could turn a malformed answer into a pass. A threshold outside 0 to 1 is refused when the adapter is built.
- **I (frames or a face template leaking through the new store):** a row holds the idempotency key as SHA-256 per method and the request (the files' storage keys and SHA-256s, in order, and the mode) as an HMAC-SHA-256 under a key derived from the method and the idempotency key; no image, frame, template, embedding or storage reference is kept, in clear or as an unkeyed hash. The result kept is the port's answer (score, pass, threshold, mode, the evidence; REGULATED). Forced RLS by tenant; the ledger names the row, the method, the key's hash, the mode and the state only. Answers and errors carry no Aadhaar-like number (the contract sweeps them with `findAadhaarLike`).
- **I / R (what the vendor keeps):** `KYC_ADAPTER_RETENTION` states, per adapter, whether the vendor keeps a face template or embedding beyond the call (CF para 14(3)), with the source; Digio and IDfy are `unknown` [VERIFY] until each gives a written statement (#148). A statement only: what is sent to a vendor, and what the platform keeps or deletes, is unchanged.
- **T (a retry served another request's answer, or two answers for one key):** a record is found by tenant, adapter, method and key hash; other frames, another mode or other images under the key is `IPB-PORT-004` (`idempotency_conflict`), checked before any vendor call. A record moves once, from pending to done (a trigger); its method, mode and binding cannot change. `kycMethodRouter` keeps a keyed check with an unavailable adapter, so frames are not sent to a second vendor for one key.
- **D (a refused mode as an outage):** `mode_unsupported` does not trip the breaker (`tripsBreaker`, R1-D-196), so a product asking for a mode an adapter lacks cannot open the KYC circuit; the router moves on to the next adapter in the method's order. **Retention of the record:** 24 hours (IPB-10-07's window, as #144), deleted on reuse or by `purgeExpired` (ledgered).

### 3.141 The RE-hosted V-CIP launcher and its signed hand-off (`KycPort.launchVcip`, `VcipHandoffContext`, `VcipConductor`, `VCIP_REASON_CODES`; PR-UDF-045, R1-D-198)

- **S (a forged or borrowed hand-off):** the LSP signs the context with `signRegulatedMessage` under its KMS key (ES256, `kid`, `iat`, `jti`; G5, no new cryptography) and the RE side verifies it with `verifyRegulatedMessage` against the key ring of the LSP the context names (`issuers` by `lspId`), so one LSP's key cannot vouch for another's customers; an unknown LSP, an untrusted key or another RE is refused `handoff_invalid` before any vendor call or stored row. The call's tenant (when given) must be the context's.
- **T (a hand-off altered in transit):** the JWS is detached, so its signature is over the context presented: a changed field, a JWS over another context or a tampered signature fails it (`handoff_invalid`). The context is strict, bounded and checked again after the signature (`vcipHandoffTimeRefusal`).
- **R / replay (a hand-off used twice or late):** a verified `jti` is claimed in the replay store (durable and shared in production, as for DPoP), so a second use is `handoff_replayed`; only a verified message burns its `jti`, so a forgery cannot spend a real hand-off. A context lives at most 15 minutes and its signature must be fresh within the `iat` window (five minutes): a late one is `handoff_expired`, and a launched session no official starts lapses as `expired` (`launch_expired`) and cannot be started.
- **I (identity data crossing to the RE or back to the LSP):** the context carries ids and times only (no name, number or document; a field with a 12-digit run or an Aadhaar-like number is refused); the launch address is `https:` on the RE's exact hosts, with no user information and no number (`vcipLaunchUrlAllowed`). The manifest the LSP reads carries a `VCIP_REASON_CODES` code; an official's free text stays in the record, deprecated and never shown, and the conductor refuses or drops one holding an identity number. A manifest sweep (`findAadhaarLike`) is in the contract. `vcipStatus` reads under the call's tenant.
- **E (an LSP acting as the RE):** the LSP only hands over; the session is the RE's (`VcipConductor` on the RE's side), started by the RE's official (`startVcip({ …, vcipId })`, for the hand-off's customer only) and decided by that official as before (para 27(3)). **D:** a refused hand-off costs a parse, one signature check and, when verified, one replay claim; the router never moves a launch to another adapter, so one hand-off cannot schedule two sessions.

### 3.142 V-CIP webhooks verified without a vendor call (`KycPort.verifyWebhook`, `verifyKycWebhookWith`, `verifyStandardWebhook`, `standardWebhookKycVerifier`; PR-UDF-046, R1-D-199)

- **S (a forged provider event):** a delivery counts only with a Standard Webhooks `v1` signature (HMAC-SHA-256 over the delivery id, timestamp and raw body, C-13) that verifies in constant time with one of the provider's secrets from SECRETS (two during rotation); `v1a` is not read. Anything else is refused `IPB-API-018` (401) before the body is parsed and before any vendor call, so a forgery costs no read-back and reaches no queue. RFC 9421 is not approved (owner answer #150, R1-D-210: C-13 covers Standard Webhooks v1 only), and Digio and IDfy refuse `verifyWebhook` until their schemes are known.
- **T (an event altered in transit):** the signature covers the id, the timestamp and the exact bytes received, so a changed body, id or timestamp fails it (`signature_invalid`). A verified event is still only a trigger: the product reads the session back with `vcipStatus` (IPB-10-27), and only that is trusted.
- **R / replay (an old or repeated delivery):** a timestamp more than five minutes away either way is `timestamp_stale`; a verified delivery's id and timestamp are claimed in the replay store (durable and shared in production), so the same delivery twice is `event_replayed`, and only a verified delivery is claimed, so a forgery cannot spend a real id. A provider's retry (same id, new timestamp and signature) verifies again and the product's module inbox de-duplicates it by `eventId`.
- **I (identity data in the receipt or the refusal):** the receipt holds the delivery id, the session id (letters, digits and `. _ : -`, no 12-digit run or Aadhaar-like number, else `malformed_event`) and a time; a refusal carries a fixed label and never the body. The contract sweeps both with `findAadhaarLike`.
- **D (a flood of large or forged deliveries):** a body over 64 KiB is refused before it is read or a secret is fetched; a forged one costs one HMAC per secret and signature (at most two and eight); a signature header over 4 KiB is refused unread; nothing calls the vendor, so the product can answer within 2 s.
- **E:** none: the verifier decides nothing about the customer, and the official's decision is unchanged.

### 3.143 The approvals a checker may check (`ApprovalsEngine.listCheckable`, `CheckableApproval`, `tenancy/0025`; PR-UDF-047, R1-D-200)

- **E (a list that widens who may check):** the list only lists; it decides nothing, and `decide` checks every rule again when the checker acts. It keeps a request only when `decide`'s own private `checkerRefusal` (the action's checker roles after the tenant's tighten-only narrowing, its checker functions, the open level's roles) has no refusal, so the two cannot drift; the maker and anyone who has already decided are left out in the query, and an agent gets an empty list (IPB-02-10). No policy, level or decision path changes.
- **I (the sealed payload or the change leaking through the list):** the query selects only the id, action, summary, risk class, maker, times, level and counts; never `payload`, `payload_enc`, the digest, the data class or the diff, and a test asserts each item's exact keys. A person who may not check a request does not learn it exists from the list (they may still read it by id with `get`, as before). Another tenant's requests never appear: the read runs through the kernel under forced RLS on both approvals tables.
- **T / S (a crafted cursor):** the cursor is only a position (the last listed item's `created_at` and id, which the caller has seen); only its canonical base64url form with a real calendar time and an `apr_` id is accepted, anything else is refused before any read, and it is passed to the database as bound parameters. A cursor taken to another tenant positions that tenant's rows only. **D:** `limit` is 1 to 100; the action rule runs once per action per call; the keyset query reads a partial index of pending, unexpired requests (`tenancy/0025`, built concurrently, so writes continue while it builds). **R:** a read writes no ledger entry, as `get`.

### 3.144 Bank statement analysis (`StatementAnalysisPort`, `platform-statement`, `http-statement`, `connectors/0014` `connectors.statement_analysis`; PR-UDF-048, R1-D-202, ADR-0012)

- **I (statements leaving India or reaching a weaker store):** the port is REGULATED by default (`PORT_DEFAULT_DATA_CLASS`), every request field is classed REGULATED, and the router's method table names `analyse`'s payload, so the router refuses an instance that cannot carry REGULATED (`IPB-PORT-003`, `ResidencyViolationBlocked`) and a routing entry that disagrees with the adapter's descriptor (`IPB-PORT-005`) before any I/O. Each adapter declares `IN` and REGULATED and refuses, before its store or analyser, a call class other than REGULATED and an input outside the REGULATED bucket class (`IPB-PORT-003` `residency`); the report is written only to the REGULATED class under the request's prefix, in the inputs' tenant (the schema refuses another tenant's keys). Nothing fails over to a secondary. The HTTP alternative talks to one configured host only (the egress allow-list, HTTPS, no redirects), which must be an analyser processing in India under a DPA (#152); it is off and unproven.
- **I (content or identity in answers, records or the ledger):** the port carries STORAGE references and SHA-256s only; the answer is cut to its keys (`statementAnalysisResultOnly`); the in-platform report is a checksum summary (FI type, digest, length, format) with nothing from a statement; the subject reference never leaves the platform (not sent to the HTTP vendor) and may hold no Aadhaar-like number. A record holds the key as SHA-256 and the request as a SHA-256 of its canonical form, which binds the statements' own digests, so it cannot be tested against guessed subjects or keys without the statements; no input key, subject or statement is kept, and the ledger names the row, the adapter, the key's hash and the state only. The contract sweeps every answer and refusal with `findAadhaarLike`.
- **T (an altered statement analysed, or a report swapped):** every input's bytes are checked against its SHA-256 before the analyser sees them (`hash_mismatch`), and a missing one is refused (`input_missing`); the report's SHA-256 is computed on the bytes stored, and a product verifies it on read. A record keeps its binding (tenant, adapter, key, request, prefix) and moves forward only, its vendor reference set once (a trigger); a final answer is never overwritten.
- **T / R (one key, two analyses or another request's answer):** a keyed retry answers the first analysis with one analyser call; the key with another subject, statements or prefix is `IPB-PORT-004` before any analyser call; a call that left no answer is resent with the same analysis id as the vendor's reference (the vendor is expected to de-duplicate on it [VERIFY]). Another tenant can neither read a record nor collide with its key (forced RLS).
- **S (a forged notification):** no approved scheme fits an unnamed vendor (C-13 names its vendors; Standard Webhooks waits for #150), so the real adapters refuse `handleWebhook` `unsupported` and a product polls `status`; no unverified notification is ever processed. The fake verifies the approved C-22 profile (ES256, `kid`, `iat` window, `jti` replay store) and trusts only the state read back. **D:** at most 24 inputs; the HTTP adapter's responses are capped at 16 MiB with a 30 s timeout; a create is never retried by the egress client. **Retention:** a record lives 24 hours (IPB-10-07), deleted on reuse or by `purgeExpired` (ledgered); statements and reports stay under the product's STORAGE retention.

### 3.145 A product's own consent artefact beside a port's consent receipt (`ConsentArtefact`, `BureauPullRequest.consentArtefact`, `AaConsentRequest.consentArtefact`; PR-UDF-049, R1-D-203, R1-D-209)

- **E / R (a pull without a consent, or a consent the platform cannot see):** `consentRef` is unchanged: the port still refuses a pull or consent request without the product's `cpr_` receipt (owner answer #153). The optional artefact beside it is checked for its shape only: the platform cannot look it up, so the product asserts the consent behind it (granted, for the purpose and the RE, unexpired, not withdrawn), as it already checks a `cpr_` with `PurposeConsents` before the pull. The artefact is echoed on the report (`consentArtefact`, beside `consentRef`) and kept in the adapter's pull record, so the product's ledger entry for the pull names the artefact it relied on and an auditor can trace it in the product's consent ledger. An artefact cannot name a platform artefact (`cpr`/`cns` kinds and ids refused), so the two never blur.
- **I (personal data in the artefact):** an artefact is a label and a bounded identifier: no `@`, spaces or `+` (no e-mail or formatted phone), no standalone run of 10 or more digits, no Aadhaar-like number in any reading `findAadhaarLike` makes, no standalone PAN-shaped run; a refusal carries a fixed label, never the value, and the contract sweeps every answer and refusal for Aadhaar-like numbers. Neither the receipt nor the artefact is sent to the CIC or the AA (the CIBIL and Setu legs assert their requests).
- **T (an artefact read back as another):** the record keeps `consentRef` and `consentArtefact` as separate fields, and `consentInForce`'s text (`<kind>:<id>`, else the `cpr_`) is unambiguous (neither part holds a colon, a `cpr_` none). A call without an artefact answers exactly as before. **D:** both parts are bounded (64 and 128 characters) and the checks are linear.

### 3.146 The AA consent's FIU check and approval link (`AaConsentRequest.fiuId`, `AaConsent.fiuId`, `AaConsent.approvalUrl`, `aaFiuMismatch`, `aaApprovalUrlAllowed`, `setu-aa`; PR-UDF-050, R1-D-204)

- **I / E (FI shared with the wrong FIU):** when the product names its RE's FIU, a consent the AA reports for another FIU, or for none, is refused `fiu_mismatch` (definite) on the AA's own read-back before the adapter keeps a record or returns the consent, so the product never gets a link to show or an id to fetch under; the asked FIU is kept with the consent and every later read-back (status, revoke, fetch, notification) is refused the same way, so a consent the AA moves to another FIU fetches nothing. The comparison is exact. A request without `fiuId` is not compared (backward compatible, #154), so a product should always pass it. The refused consent stays PENDING and unapproved at the AA and lapses; no data can flow without the customer's approval.
- **S / I (a link that sends the customer elsewhere, or leaks):** the approval link is answered only while PENDING and only after `aaApprovalUrlAllowed`: `https:`, no user information, an exact host from the adapter's `approvalHosts` (no wildcard or suffix), bounded length, and no 12-digit run or Aadhaar-like number once every standalone RFC 9562 UUID is set aside (an AA's consent and link ids are UUIDs and may end in twelve decimal digits; owner answer #154; a UUID in the host is not set aside for the host match, and a 12-digit run anywhere else is still refused); otherwise the consent is refused `approval_url_not_allowed` before anything is kept. The link is never logged and never put in an error (the tests assert the refusals hold no link), and the product shows it to the customer only (never in an event). An AA's link lets the holder reach the customer's approval page, where the customer signs in at the AA [VERIFY], so it is handled as CONFIDENTIAL.
- **I (the product's references):** the consent receipt and the product's own artefact (§3.145) and the FIU id are never sent to Setu (the leg asserts its requests); Setu records the FIU behind the product instance. **T:** a notification is still never trusted; the consent, its FIU included, is read back from the AA. **D:** the create now reads the consent back once before answering (one more request), with the egress client's existing limits.

### 3.147 Work Inbox snooze (`InboxService.snooze`, `unsnooze`, `workflow/0005` `workflow.inbox_snooze`; PR-LDC-46, R1-D-206)

- **E (hiding work from someone else):** a snooze is per person: it hides the item from the principal who wrote it and from no one else, so a role item stays in front of every other holder. Only a principal who already sees the item may snooze it, by the inbox's existing rule (the user assignee or a holder of the assignee role, never the excluded maker); anything else answers `NotFoundError`, so the call discloses no item. No role check or policy is added.
- **D / R (a due item or an escalation lost from view):** a snooze ends no later than the item's due time (a later one is refused), is at most 30 days (in the service and as a table CHECK), and is refused on an escalated item or an escalation; every read also treats a snooze as over once the item is escalated or past due, so even a snooze written earlier never hides it. Escalation timers do not read snoozes, so a snoozed item still escalates on time. Reappearance needs no timer or poller: each read compares the time with `now()`. Snooze and unsnooze are ledgered in the same transaction; unsnooze sets `cleared_at` (no delete grant), so the history stays.
- **I (tenancy):** forced RLS by tenant on the new table, as `inbox_item`; ids and times only (INTERNAL), no title or personal data.

### 3.148 The `toXlsx` layout options and typed formula cells (`xlsxFormula`, `XlsxSheet.conditionalFormats`, `page`, `rowLayout`; PR-LDC-52, R1-D-207)

- **T / E (formula injection, CWE-1236):** text from data is never a formula: string cells keep `neutraliseFormula` and are written as inline strings, and a formula is only a value `xlsxFormula` made in the same process (a module-private `WeakSet`), so a string, a JSON round-trip or a look-alike object from data is written as text or refused by its column. The product writes the formula text, so a product that builds one from user text must quote it itself; the writer still refuses, outside string literals, `[` and `]` (external workbook references), `|` (DDE, the classic `cmd|' /C …'!A0`) and the functions that fetch, link, run code or reveal the workbook's path and environment (`WEBSERVICE`, `IMAGE`, `HYPERLINK`, `RTD`, `CALL`, `REGISTER`, `REGISTER.ID`, `EXEC`, `STOCKHISTORY`, `INFO`, `CELL` (owner answer #156), with or without `_xlfn.`), and a leading `=`. The list is a deny list (#156); conditional formats take no expression at all: the writer builds each from typed values, with text quoted (quotes doubled) and SEARCH wildcards escaped.
- **I (exfiltration on open):** with no external reference, DDE or fetching function, a formula can read only the workbook itself; recalculation on load (`fullCalcOnLoad`) therefore reaches nothing outside it. Print names and filters are sheet-local defined names the writer makes; a caller's named range is checked as before.
- **D:** every new element is bounded (outline 0 to 7, colours six hex digits, formulas 8 192 characters, conditional values 255 characters, print areas inside the grid) and counts against the existing uncompressed byte cap.

### 3.149 The origination contract and the mock lender (`contracts` `events/origination.ts`, `fakes` `FakeLender`, `crypto` `localRegulatedSigner`; PR-UDF-051, R1-D-217)

- **S / T (a forged or altered lender decision, KFS, sanction letter or disbursal):** the four are regulated messages; an LSP accepts one only with a detached JWS that verifies against the lender's pinned key ring (`verifyRegulatedMessage`: ES256 only, `kid`, `iat` window, `jti` replay). The contract cases prove a JWS fails with another key under the same `kid` and after any data change. RFC 9421 in transit is not offered (not approved, #150).
- **E (a test key in production):** `localRegulatedSigner` makes an in-memory key for tests and fakes; its doc and the guide say a real producer signs with its KMS key (C-22). `FakeLender` emits `environment: "test"` envelopes only, and its `kid` is not one any production key ring lists.
- **I (personal data in the journey's messages):** applicants travel by reference only, documents by id and SHA-256, evidence by `storage:` key or https URI with its hash; the cases sweep every message for Aadhaar-like numbers. Money is integer paise, never a float. **R:** every message names its Specification idempotency key in `subject`, so an LSP's inbox de-duplicates redeliveries.
- **D:** the eligibility call is bounded by the 3 s budget and an idempotency key; a changed body under a used key is refused.

### 3.150 Documents by reference, eSign and eStamp evidence and the execution pack (`contracts` `events/documents.ts`, `crypto` `signDocumentJws`, `fakes` `FakeLender`; PR-UDF-053, R1-D-219)

- **T (a document swapped or altered in transfer):** a document travels by reference with a detached document JWS (the C-22 profile, `typ: ipb-document+jws`) over `{ document_id, version, sha256 }`; the consumer verifies it against the producer's pinned keys before download and the bytes against `sha256` after. The JWS binds identity and version, so a valid document cannot be presented as another one. No new primitive (owner question #158).
- **I (a document URL leaking):** `GET /v1/documents/{id}` answers an `https` URL living at most 15 minutes (`DocumentRetrieval` refuses longer), hosted in India (`data_residency: "IN"`); the URL is a bearer capability, so it is never logged or put in an event, only in the call's answer. **S / R (forged eSign or eStamp evidence):** `ESignCompleted` and `EStampCompleted` are regulated messages; the lender refuses one without a JWS, with one that does not verify against the LSP's key ring, or with a reused `jti` (`signature-invalid`), and refuses evidence for a document it never shared. `ESignCompleted` requires the ESP transaction, the certificate summary and the signed hash, and a signing time inside the certificate's validity: "never a bare flag".
- **E (a test key in production):** the fake lender's document and message keys and `lspSigner()` are in-memory test keys (§3.149); a real producer signs with its KMS key.
- **Residual:** the execution-pack manifest has no Specification carrier yet; until PR-047 is decided it travels as a JSON document with the same JWS, so its integrity is covered but its schema is a platform convention, not a Specification contract.

### 3.156 Named tenant keys in STORAGE (`ObjectMeta.keyRef`, `S3StorageOptions.tenantNamedKmsKeyIds`, `tenantKeyRefPrefixes`, `resolveStorageKeyRef`; PR-UDF-058, R1-D-227)

- **E / T (a caller choosing the key):** a caller names a reference only; the adapter's configuration maps it to a key id within the object key's tenant (itself tied to the caller's tenant), and a reference shaped like an ARN, `mrk-` id or UUID is refused, so no caller can direct an object to an arbitrary or another tenant's key. A caller's tag cannot claim a reference: the adapter alone writes `ipb-key-ref`.
- **I (key sharing):** as PR-LDC-60, a named key equal to a class or compartment key, to another reference's key, or (in a compartment) to the same reference's key elsewhere is refused, so revoking one lender's key never touches another lender's or the platform's objects. A tenant with its own CMK cannot use named keys (C-17's purge covers all its objects). Named keys only in `CONFIDENTIAL` and `REGULATED` (and, from PR-UDF-066, `QUARANTINE`: §3.164): audit anchors and public assets stay on platform keys.
- **D (a lender revokes or disables its key):** reads and writes of that lender's objects fail with `IPB-KMS-001` `storage_key_unavailable` (no fallback, no generic 500); objects under platform keys, and transient KMS errors, keep `IPB-PORT-002` and the router's read failover. Retained objects stay locked but unreadable until the key returns; the guide says to settle this in the lender contract. With S3 Bucket Keys on, a revocation takes effect when S3's bucket-level data key expires (owner question #165).
- **R:** the reference (not the key id) is recorded on each object and returned by `get`, so audit and export show which key protected it; the lender's own CloudTrail records the platform's use of its key. An overwrite cannot change an object's reference (`key_ref_changed`).
- **I (disclosure of key ids):** an upload URL signs the key-id header, so the uploader is given the key id (as for compartments); a KMS key id is an identifier, not a secret, and the key policy still limits its use to the platform's role.
- **D / R (Bucket Key off, R1-D-229):** a named key configured `{ keyId, bucketKey: false }` writes its objects without S3 Bucket Keys, so a lender's revocation takes effect immediately rather than when the bucket-level data key expires, and the lender's CloudTrail shows a KMS call per object operation; the price is one KMS request per object read and write (a recurring cost). Default stays on; the choice is per lender, with its key policy.

### 3.165 Text extraction from a file under a revoked named key (`connectors` `PdfTextExtractor`; PR-UDF-067, R1-D-241)

- **I (key detail in the error):** when the STORAGE read is refused with `IPB-KMS-001` `storage_key_unavailable` (§3.156), `extractText` throws a fresh `IPB-KMS-001` whose `meta` is that fixed label only: not the object's key reference, the vendor's error name, the key id or ARN, nor the STORAGE error as `cause` (an AWS message can name the key ARN). The detail is a fixed sentence. Tested with a STORAGE fake whose error carries a reference, a vendor name and an ARN in its cause.
- **D (a revoked key read as an outage):** before, the refusal was wrapped as `IPB-PORT-002` `unavailable`, so a caller could retry a read that cannot succeed until the lender restores its grant; now it sees the 503 `IPB-KMS-001` with the label and can tell the user the lender's key is unavailable. Every other read failure, an `IPB-KMS-001` with another reason included, stays `IPB-PORT-002`.
- **T / E:** unchanged. The AV proof and the hash check still run on the bytes read, and nothing is read or parsed when the key is unavailable; there is no fallback to another copy or key.
- **Residual:** other readers that swallow STORAGE errors (the ClamAV adapter and `FakeFileScan` report a scan `ERROR` `unavailable`; `brand` maps a read failure to `IPB-STOR-001`) do not yet distinguish a revoked key (R1-D-241 (4)); they fail closed. No new port, adapter, setting, table or algorithm.

### 3.151 The GRC port and the §6.15 events (`GrcPort`, `generic-grc`, `FakeGrc`, `contracts` `events/grc-events.ts`; PR-FBX-51, R1-D-220, ADR-0013)

- **I (personal data reaching a GRC system):** §9.2 lets a GRC system hold references, hashes, aggregate counts and masked sample ids only. Every event schema refuses an Aadhaar-like number (`findAadhaarLike`, letters joining so digests are not read as numbers) or a PAN anywhere in the data, unknown fields included, and every adapter checks the raw body the same way before the schema and before any request (`IPB-PORT-003` `personal_data`); people are role codes or references, samples masked (`GRC_MASKED_ID`), evidence a reference with its SHA-256. The port carries at most CONFIDENTIAL (`data_class` above it), `IN` only; the obligations answer is cut to the §9.2 fields, never the text. The OH13 incident copy carries the schema's fields only (`securityIncidentClockCopy`).
- **I (egress):** the generic adapter talks to one configured host (egress allow-list, HTTPS, no redirects, 4 MiB answers, 10 s timeout), which must be India-resident; it is off and unproven until a counterparty sandbox exists. Its bearer token is read from SECRETS on every call, never from the environment; §9.2's mTLS-bound client credentials are not built yet (#159).
- **T / R (a duplicate or rewritten record):** every POST carries an `Idempotency-Key` (the caller's, else the event's own Specification key), so a resend is safe and is retried like a read; the key with another body is refused (`IPB-PORT-004`) by the fake and expected of the counterparty (`422`). An incident update at or below one taken is refused `out_of_order`, so a stale update cannot overwrite a later status. Hash-chaining of evidence stays with the producing product's audit ledger (§9.2), not this port.
- **S:** inbound GRC webhooks are not part of this port; obligations are pulled over the authenticated client. **D:** pages of at most 200 obligations; a cursor is opaque, bound to its query, and a foreign one refused (`invalid_cursor`). **Retention:** the platform keeps nothing (no migration); records live at the GRC system under its retention.
- **I / S (CERT-In fields and the client credential, R1-D-229):** `cert_in_category` (a fixed code list) and `cert_in_report_by` (a time no later than `detected_at` + 6 h) are clock fields with no personal data, under the same personal-data refusal as the rest of the payload. The `generic-grc` bearer token is acceptable only while the adapter is off and unproven: the §9.2 mTLS-bound client credentials are required before any staging-or-above connection is enabled.

### 3.152 Staff and RE-administrator notifications by role (`notify` `notifyStaff`, `enqueueStaff`, `deliveryReceipt`; `contracts` staff classes; PR-UDF-052, R1-D-222)

- **I (personal data in a staff subject or message):** a staff class declares its variables; NOTIFY refuses (`IPB-NTF-007`) an undeclared one and any value holding an e-mail address, a phone number, a PAN or an Aadhaar number or VID (`findAadhaarLike`), before anything is recorded, and never echoes the value in the error. A subject variable must be a reference or code (no spaces, no run of 10 or more digits). In-app and push titles of staff classes may use only `subject_variables` (a finding fails boot), and a tenant's own staff email subject likewise (template lint at save and at every delivery and preview). Only in-app and email are allowed, so nothing reaches SMS, WhatsApp or push.
- **E / I (the wrong people or another tenant):** holders are found inside the recording transaction, from `tenancy.membership` and unrevoked `tenancy.function_assignment` rows under forced RLS with the tenant also named; only `active` people of type `user` (never agents or service accounts); the request has no tenant or address field, so one call never leaves the context's tenant. Platform, service and agent roles are refused. Tested: a holder of the same role in another tenant receives nothing, and another tenant cannot read the receipt.
- **R:** one delivery row per person and channel, and the ledger entry `notify.notification.create` covers the roles, the resolved ids and the outcome, `no_recipients` included; replays are ledgered.
- **T / S (sending a staff class to anyone):** `send` and `enqueue` refuse a staff class, so role resolution and the variable checks always run.
- **D:** at most 10 roles per call and 1,000 recipients (more is refused); the idempotency key makes a retry a replay.
- **Residual:** a product's vendor-side email template (`templates.email.key`) is not visible to the platform; its subject should use only `subject_variables` (guide §7dh), and the value checks bound what it could show. Detection of personal data is by pattern: a person's name in a body variable is not detected (subject variables cannot carry spaces). The operator's team is reached in the operator's own tenant; cross-tenant fan-out is not offered (#160).

### 3.153 Borrower email attachments, the delivery receipt and the verified email (`NotificationRequest.attachments`, `EmailMessage.files`, `notify/0008`, `CustomerIdentityPort.startEmailVerification`, `connectors/0015` `connectors.customer_verified_email`; PR-UDF-055, R1-D-224)

- **I (a confidential document leaving by email):** IPB-01 sends CONFIDENTIAL content by secure link. NOTIFY attaches only `GENERAL` and `PUBLIC_ASSETS` objects, never a compartment, `REGULATED`, `AUDIT` or `QUARANTINE` object; a CONFIDENTIAL one needs the platform setting `ntf.email.attachments.confidential` (off, security officer, R4) **and** an adapter that is `IN` with `maxDataClass` CONFIDENTIAL, and then the call itself is raised to CONFIDENTIAL so the adapter's own class check applies. No adapter qualifies today (SES and Brevo are INTERNAL), so the setting alone cannot weaken the rule (#161). Attachments are off per tenant until `ntf.email.attachments.enabled`, and only for urgent non-marketing classes (never a digest). **I (another tenant's document):** a key outside the caller's tenant is refused before anything is written and again at send; STORAGE keys carry the tenant.
- **T (bytes swapped or re-rendered between the store and the inbox):** the request names each document by SHA-256; NOTIFY reads the object at send and refuses (`IPB-PORT-003` `sha256`) bytes that differ, and the adapter checks the digest again over the exact bytes it hands to the vendor (SES `RawContent`, Brevo base64 of the same bytes); nothing decodes or re-encodes them. **T (active content under a document's name, IPB-02-44):** the declared type must equal the stored content type and the bytes' magic number (PDF, PNG, JPEG only), and the file name is a plain name whose extension fits the type (no path, no control characters). **R:** the delivery row keeps each sent file's SHA-256 and size with the vendor message id, written with its ledger entry in the same transaction (the entry's digested payload holds the hashes); file names, which may name a person, are kept only on the notification row (CONFIDENTIAL) and never put in a ledger payload. **D:** at most 10 files and 10 MiB together, checked by NOTIFY and by the adapter before any request; an adapter without `attachments: "native"` is refused (`unsupported`) rather than dropping files silently.
- **S (someone else's address bound to an account):** the capture is off until `int.identity.customer.email_capture.enabled`; it starts only with a valid DPoP proof by the key of a signed-in (active) device of that account and finishes only with the same key, the same account, the same address (its hash bound in the challenge) and the six-digit code (CSPRNG) sent to that address through NOTIFY. **I / T (codes):** only the code's salted SHA-256 and the address's hash are stored on the challenge; the code is never stored, logged or returned; a verification challenge is never accepted as a sign-in. **D / brute force:** the account's `auth.otp` guard applies unchanged (send limit and window, resend interval, tries per code, back-off, lockout, all `int.identity.customer.otp.*` settings), shared with sign-in codes. **I (the address at rest):** one row per account, `email` and `user_id` CONFIDENTIAL, forced RLS by tenant, written through the kernel with a ledger entry that names the account's hash and the time, never the address. **Retention (owner answer #161 (3), R1-D-233; R1-D-235):** the row is kept while the account is open, replaced by a later proof, and deleted when the account is closed (`retireLogin`) or on the borrower's erasure request (`eraseVerifiedEmail`), each deletion ledgered; the delete grant is on this table only and only the deletion's kernel action may use it (§3.159).

### 3.157 No-cache presigned downloads (`NO_CACHE_PRESIGN_CLASSES`, `PRESIGN_NO_CACHE`, `S3Storage.presign`; PR-UDF-060, R1-D-230)

- **I (device and webview caches keeping a REGULATED document):** a presigned GET from a `REGULATED` or `CONFIDENTIAL` bucket (compartments included) signs the overrides `response-cache-control=no-store, private` and a past `response-expires`, so the store's response forbids iOS, webview, browser and shared caches from keeping the document after the 300-second URL has expired. The overrides are part of the SigV4 signature and come from the bucket the URL reads from, never from the caller, so a caller cannot drop or weaken them, and an edited URL fails the signature. **Residual:** `Pragma` cannot be signed (no S3 override); HTTP/1.0 caches still honour the past `Expires`, and a client that would obey only a response `Pragma` is not covered (a product needing it proxies the bytes). Headers are advice to well-behaved clients: an app that writes the body to its own storage, or a user's screenshot or "save to Files", is outside them (the product guide says so). Other classes are unchanged.

### 3.158 Udofin's `udf_ext` v1 fields and `CoolingOffExitAnswered` (`@infrabyx/contracts/events` origination; PR-UDF-061, R1-D-232)

- **I (more personal data to the lender):** `ApplicationSubmitted.economic_profile` (age, occupation, monthly income, income source) and the household figures are CONFIDENTIAL personal data on the existing LSP-to-lender flow. They are figures and codes, never documents or identifiers; `consent_purposes` must name one of the application's `consents`, so the schema refuses a purpose answered by no consent the event carries. A product sends them only under the consent it names (DPDP purpose limitation).
- **S, T (phishing through the lender's words):** `status_text`, `reason_texts`, `penal_charges_text`, `exit_terms`, the Annex names and `payment_instruction` are free text from the lender that a product shows the borrower. Control characters other than tab and line breaks are refused, and products render them as text, never markup (guide §7dn). `payment_instruction` refuses any link (`scheme://`, `upi:`, `www.`, `domain.tld/`), so a tampered or spoofed answer cannot carry a payment page.
- **S, T (an unsigned money instruction):** `CoolingOffExitAnswered` carries an amount and how to pay it, but is not a §3.6 regulated message until the Specification lists it (owner question #166; proposed in PR-047 §5). Until then it is taken only over the lender's authenticated channel (mTLS and RFC 9421 per §3.6 transport). Products never collect the money themselves (CF para 10) and never act on the instruction automatically.
- **SSRF and content swap (`kfs_url`):** an `https` URL from the lender, without credentials or a fragment, meant for the borrower's browser. Products do not fetch it server-side; the KFS bytes come through `GET /v1/documents/{id}` (§3.150) and are checked against `KFSIssued.document_sha256`.
- **T (accepting another KFS):** `BorrowerAccepted.kfs_version` and `kfs_sha256` let the lender refuse an acceptance of a KFS other than the one it issued (`kfs_mismatch`, in the mock lender and its contract case).
- **I (device tracking):** `device_ref` is the LSP's own reference, never a hardware or advertising identifier.
- **No new port, adapter, setting, table or algorithm.**

### 3.159 Deleting the verified email at account closure or on erasure (`connectors/0016`, `CustomerAuthStore.deleteVerifiedEmail`, `CustomerIdentityPort.eraseVerifiedEmail`, `retireLogin`; owner answer #161 (3), R1-D-233, R1-D-235)

- **I (an address kept after the borrower left or asked):** `retireLogin` deletes the account's `connectors.customer_verified_email` row after its devices are forgotten and before the IdP frees the login, so a retry after an IdP failure still finds the account; `eraseVerifiedEmail` deletes it on the borrower's erasure request whether or not the capture is on. A store that can save an address but not delete it makes both refuse (`IPB-PORT-001` `unsupported`) before anything changes, so no closure or erasure silently leaves one. The platform keeps no other copy: ledger entries name the account by hash, the verification challenge keeps only the address's hash, and NOTIFY's deliveries keep a recipient reference, not the address.
- **T / E (deleting what should stay, or another tenant's row):** `connectors/0016` grants DELETE to `app_rw` on this table only; no other connectors table and no other role gains one (an integration test lists every connectors table `app_rw` may delete from). A trigger refuses every delete unless the kernel transaction's action is `identity.customer.verified_email.delete` (set by the kernel, as the consent #95 purge), so a product's own SQL under another action cannot delete the row. The forced tenant policy of `connectors/0015` scopes the delete to the caller's tenant. The kernel refuses a `delete` verb to agents (IPB-03), so a Vidhi agent cannot erase.
- **R:** each deletion and its ledger entry are one transaction (action `identity.customer.verified_email.delete`, the account's hash, `before` the verified time, `after` the reason `account_closed` or `erasure_request`, whether a row was found and the erasure request's reference); a repeated call deletes nothing, does not fail and is ledgered with `found: false`, so the request's handling is on the record. A product that runs its closure or erasure in an ambient kernel transaction gets the deletion joined to it.
- **Residual:** the address may still sit in the email vendor's logs (under the vendor's retention) and in any copy the product keeps itself, which the product's erasure workflow handles; the borrower can prove an address again after an erasure, which records a fresh row. A login retired because the operator reports the number recycled also deletes the row, as the account is disabled (owner question #167).

### 3.160 The signed execution pack, the v2 document JWS and per-subject envelopes (`ExecutionPackIssued`, `signDocumentJwsV2`, `originationSubject`; PR-UDF-061 items 12 to 17, PR-047, R1-D-236)

- **S, T (an execution pack or an exit answer from someone else, or changed on the way):** `ExecutionPackIssued` and `CoolingOffExitAnswered` are now §3.6 regulated messages (owner's approval of PR-047, 20 Nov 2026): a consumer verifies the detached JWS against the lender's pinned keys and refuses an unsigned or wrongly signed one with `signature-invalid` before acting. This closes §3.158's unsigned money instruction; `payment_instruction` still refuses any link.
- **T (an invalid execution):** the pack tells the LSP how each document is executed. The schema refuses eSign or DSC for an IT Act First Schedule instrument (a promissory note, a power of attorney, …), an executed document that names no instrument or no signer, a signer outside the four roles, stamp evidence on a document that needs none or in another State than the pack's, deficient stamping (duty paid below the duty stated), a quoted enclosure no document furnishes, and a KFS other than the one the borrower accepted (`kfs_sha256`). A business-purpose loan without the declared wilful-defaulter covenant is refused.
- **T (a document relabelled, moved to another application or swapped for one of another size):** the v2 document JWS (`typ: ipb-document-v2+jws`) also binds `type`, `media_type`, `size_bytes` and `application_id`, so a tampered `DocumentShared` or retrieval answer fails verification before the download, and the consumer checks the bytes' length as well as their SHA-256. **Downgrade:** a v1 JWS still verifies (older producers) and binds only id, version and hash; a consumer sets `requireV2` once its lender signs v2 (owner question #168). Same C-22 profile, key and ES256: no new primitive, key type or algorithm (crypto registry entry added).
- **D, T (stuck or misordered inboxes):** the envelope `subject` is now the aggregate id (the application for its origination events, the loan for servicing) and `seq` counts per subject (§3.2, §3.5), so one application's decision, KFS, sanction letter and pack arrive as one ordered stream and a consumer's gap parking and replay work per aggregate; a lender-wide counter read per subject would have looked like permanent gaps.
- **I:** no new personal data: the pack carries references, codes, hashes and amounts; `language` is a BCP 47 tag; the mock lender's PDFs are synthetic, say so on their first page, and are test only.
- **No new port, adapter, setting, table or migration.**

### 3.161 Another principal, another tenant and queued calls in an ambient mutation (`PgKernel.ambient`; PR-UDF-062, R1-D-237)

Supersedes the "refused" parts of §3.58's joined-mutation bullet and §3.133's T bullet: another principal of the tenant now joins, another tenant's call runs on its own, and a concurrent sibling waits instead of being refused.

- **S, R (a write or ledger entry under another principal):** a joined call by another principal runs in a kernel savepoint with its own `app.*` settings, so `app.actor_id` is its own while it runs: RLS policies that read the actor, the ledger's insert policy (`actor_id = app.actor_id`) and the statutory capture trigger all see that principal, and its ledger entries name it. The settings of the call it was made from are put back on every path (released, rolled back or refused) before any other call may use the connection; a joined read's settings, role and read-only flag go with its always-rolled-back savepoint (§3.133). Tested: inside the joined call the actor is its own; afterwards a statement in the ambient transaction sees the ambient principal again, after a throw and after an SQL error; an insert naming another actor is refused (`42501`); ledger rows name the principal that wrote them; a nested call restores the outer joined call's settings.
- **E (a grant or gate skipped by joining):** another principal is refused when it or the ambient call carries a break-glass grant or support access, or it is an external principal or a service reader, because their own transactions run grant and gate checks (break-glass preflight, support grant, external grant, reader roles) that a joined call does not. The ambient call's scope and the app role are still required; agent and impersonation guards (`assertMayMutate`) run before any call joins.
- **I (another tenant's uncommitted rows):** another tenant's `mutate` or `read` is never joined: it runs as if no ambient mutation were open, in its own transaction on another connection, so RLS keys it to its own tenant and neither transaction sees the other's uncommitted rows (tested both ways on a table without RLS). A call nested inside it does not join the ambient mutation either. Residual: it needs a second pool connection (owner question #169).
- **T (interleaved statements on one connection):** joined calls queue in a per-ambient FIFO and run one at a time; only the innermost open call may send statements. The ambient callback's own statements and units wait while a joined call runs (`PgTx.turn`), so none runs inside another call's savepoint or under its settings; the wait and the statement are sent in one tick. A call made from inside a running joined call runs at once (nesting never queues behind its caller). A call or statement left behind by a joined call whose callback has settled is refused. Tested: the order of a `Promise.all` of three mutations and a read; a failure in one queued call leaves the others' settings and rows intact; the exact statement sequence (one savepoint at a time, each call's settings, the ambient's after each).
- **D (a queue that never drains):** a joined call that awaits a sibling queued after it waits for ever, as with any lock (#169). The ambient mutation still waits for every joined call before it ends and fails if one was running (R1-D-92).
- **T (settings that cannot be restored):** if the restore or a joined read's rollback fails, the ambient mutation is marked broken: queued calls and later statements are refused, nothing more is sent, and it rolls back even when the caller caught the error (tested without a database).
- **No new port, adapter, setting, table or algorithm.**

### 3.162 Partner mTLS client certificates and the Standard Webhooks receiver (`crypto` `readClientCertificate`, `PinnedCaSet`, `StandardWebhookReceiver`, `mtls-test`; `docs/platform/mtls-edge-header.md`; PR-UDF-064, R1-D-238)

- **S (a caller forging the edge header):** behind an edge the certificate arrives in `X-IPB-Client-Cert` (or Envoy's XFCC), which any client could also send. Control: the contract (`docs/platform/mtls-edge-header.md` §3) requires the edge to verify the certificate in a mode that ends a failed handshake, to strip every client copy of the header on every listener, to set it only on the partner mTLS routes, and to be the only network path to the product's partner listener; the product trusts the header by network position only. Where a product cannot guarantee the path, it terminates TLS itself (`{ socket }`, refused `tls_unauthorized` unless the TLS layer authorised the peer) or adds the pinned-CA check. **Residual:** a misconfigured edge that forwards a client's header is outside the platform's code; the pinned-CA check limits it to certificates a pinned CA issued.
- **S (a certificate from the wrong CA, or the same name on another key):** the optional `PinnedCaSet` checks the issuer by name (`checkIssued`) and by signature (`verify` with the CA's key), directly or through at most two configured intermediates, each a CA and valid now; intermediates come from configuration only, never from the request. Authorisation is on `x5t#S256` (RFC 8705), never on the subject. Issuer signatures other than ECDSA P-256 / SHA-256 are refused `issuer_algorithm` (owner question #170), so no algorithm outside the approved `ecdsa-p256-sha256` entry is relied on. **Residual:** no CRL, OCSP, name-constraint, policy or path-length processing; revocation is by removing the `x5t#S256` from the product's partner registry and the CA from the edge trust store.
- **T (ambiguous input):** exactly one leaf: two header values, two PEM blocks, a forwarded chain or two XFCC elements are `multiple_certificates`; any text or key beside the certificate, non-canonical base64 or trailing DER bytes are `unparsable`; percent-decoding only, so `+` is never read as a space. A CA certificate, or one whose extended key usage excludes client authentication, is `not_client_certificate`. Validity is checked against the caller's clock (`not_yet_valid`, `expired`).
- **I (certificate contents in logs):** nothing logs; a refusal is a fixed label and never carries the header, the PEM or a name. Products log `x5tS256`, `serialNumber`, `notAfter` and the label only.
- **D (oversize headers):** values over 16 KiB are refused `too_large` before decoding; the XFCC parser is linear and bounded by the same limit; at most 32 pinned CAs and 8 intermediates, parsed once at configuration.
- **S, T, R (webhooks):** `StandardWebhookReceiver` adds no cryptography to `verifyStandardWebhook` (C-13, #150: HMAC-SHA-256, constant time, `v1` only): the body limit first (64 KiB default, 1 MiB at most), the three headers (a repeated one is `headers_malformed`), one or two secrets read from SECRETS per delivery (three or more throw), a window of at most 300 s, and then the replay claim, made only after the signature verified so a forgery cannot burn a real delivery. The default `delivery` key (`webhook-id` with its signed timestamp, as R1-D-199) refuses a captured delivery sent again, while a sender's signed retry verifies and the module inbox de-duplicates it (IPB-10-27); `event` refuses the same id inside the window too. In production the replay store is durable and shared (`RedisReplayStore`); `MemoryReplayStore` is for tests and one instance. **Residual:** with the `delivery` key, a product without an inbox could process a signed retry twice; such a product uses `event`.
- **Tests only:** `@infrabyx/crypto/mtls-test` signs throwaway certificates with in-memory P-256 keys (P-384 only to prove the `issuer_algorithm` refusal); no key is committed, and nothing it issues chains to a real CA.
- **No new port, adapter, setting, table or algorithm.** New uses are recorded under the approved `sha-256` and `ecdsa-p256-sha256` registry entries.

### 3.163 Named tenant key status (`StoragePort.keyRefStatus`, `S3Storage.keyRefStatus`, `storageKeyRefStatusRefusal`; PR-UDF-068, R1-D-239)

- **I (disclosure of key ids and key configuration):** the answer is a fixed label (`resolves`, a write's refusal label or `storage_key_unavailable`) with at most a KMS key state; the key id or ARN never appears in the answer, an error or its cause (a transient `DescribeKey` failure is rethrown as `IPB-PORT-002` with only the error's name and status, since a KMS message can name the ARN). A caller learns only what a write would tell it.
- **E / I (probing another tenant's keys):** the tenant must be the caller's (`currentTenant`, `IPB-PORT-003` otherwise), a tenant id must have the storage-key form, and a reference resolves only within its own tenant (`key_ref_unknown` elsewhere), so the check cannot enumerate another tenant's or lender's references.
- **T (a status that disagrees with the write):** the status runs the write's own checks (the shared `resolveStorageKeyRef` rules and `namedKeyFor`), so it cannot answer `resolves` for a reference a write would refuse on configuration; `minio`, where every named-key write is refused, declares it `unsupported`; the router never fails it over to the DR store, whose keys are not the write's. **Residual:** it is a point-in-time answer: a key disabled, a grant revoked or configuration changed after the check still fails the later write or read with `IPB-KMS-001` `storage_key_unavailable`, which products must handle anyway. Object-level refusals (`key_ref_changed`, `key_ref_prefix_conflict`) are not checked.
- **D (KMS load):** without `describeKey` no S3 or KMS call is made. With it, one `DescribeKey` per call (KMS request quotas apply; no new recurring cost beyond KMS requests, which products should not issue per upload when a cached answer will do). A key the role may not describe answers `storage_key_unavailable` (fail closed), so the lender's key policy must also grant `kms:DescribeKey` to the STORAGE role (production configuration, owner question #171).
- **R:** a read of configuration, not ledgered (STORAGE adapters write no ledger); a product that acts on the answer ledgers its own decision.
- **No new port, adapter, setting, table, dependency or algorithm.**

### 3.164 Named tenant keys in QUARANTINE (`NAMED_KEY_CLASSES`, `promotedObjectMeta`; PR-UDF-066, R1-D-240)

- **I (an upload under a platform key before its scan):** a lender's upload may now be written to `QUARANTINE` under the lender's named key (by reference or prefix map), so its bytes are never under a key shared with other lenders, even before the scan. The separation rules of §3.156 apply unchanged (a named key equal to the `QUARANTINE` class key is `key_ref_shared`).
- **E / I (the AV engine's decrypt grant):** the ClamAV adapter reads the object through STORAGE, so the role of the workload running it needs `kms:Decrypt` on each named key used in `QUARANTINE`; clamd itself has no S3 or KMS access. That widens who can decrypt the lender's key. If one key serves `QUARANTINE` and the clean classes (the default, #172), the grant could also decrypt the lender's clean objects; the guide's key policy statement limits it with `kms:ViaService` S3 and `kms:EncryptionContext:aws:s3:arn` on the quarantine bucket, and the scanner role gets `s3:GetObject` on that bucket only. The grant is production configuration; the reference modules manage no scanner role or per-lender key, so none was changed.
- **T / I (a promotion falling back to the class key):** the platform has no promotion method; `promotedObjectMeta` carries the reference into the product's clean write, which is then under that reference's key at the clean location or refused (`key_ref_unknown`, `key_ref_class`), never under the clean class's key; a prefix map covering both keys gives both writes the reference unasked. **Residual:** a product that builds the clean meta itself, drops `keyRef` and uses no prefix map writes the copy under the class key; the adapter cannot tell a copy from a new object. The guide requires the helper or a prefix map.
- **D (a lender stopping the scan):** a revoked, disabled or ungranted key makes the scanner's read `IPB-KMS-001` and the scan `ERROR` (`unavailable`), so the file stays quarantined (fail closed, tested) and is never released unscanned. A lender-controlled key therefore lets the lender block its own uploads; the guide says to agree this in the lender contract (as §3.156 for retained files).
- **No new port, adapter, setting, table, dependency, algorithm or IAM change.**

### 3.166 The no-Aadhaar sweep's gaps closed (`isAadhaarFieldName`, `findAadhaarNamedFields`, `FindAadhaarOptions.allow`, `sweepDatabaseForAadhaar`, `infrabyx-aadhaar-scan` exit 2, the idempotent `TestDatabase.end()`; PR-UDF-063, R1-D-242)

- **I (an Aadhaar number under a name, or in a column the sweep did not read):** §3.138's sweep read text-like columns only and judged values by the number rule alone. It now reads every column of every type (each cast to its text form, so `numeric` keeps every digit and `json` keeps duplicate keys as written; `bytea` as hex and as decoded bytes), and a column or JSON field named like an Aadhaar carrier (`aadhaar`, `aadhaar_number`, `uid`, `uid_token` and the listed variants, any case and separators) is a `FIELD` find whenever it holds a value, whatever the value: a name is evidence on its own, a check digit is not needed. The platform's own migrations have no such column (checked for this change); `aadhaar_last_digits` and `uid_kind` are not on the list.
- **I (the finds):** a `FIELD` find carries schema, table, column, `ctid`, the JSON key path and `(value withheld)`; never the value. Number finds stay masked (§3.138).
- **T (a sweep that proves nothing passing as clean):** the CLI exits 2, not 0, when it read no file (empty or skipped-only paths) or a file could not be read (before: 1, which also failed but read as a find); the database sweep rejects when it read no table or no row (`AadhaarScanEmptyError`; `allowEmpty` is the caller's explicit opt-out) and past `maxRows` (`AadhaarScanLimitError`, default 100 000), never stopping silently on a partial read.
- **T (an identifier hiding a number):** a UUID is blanked only when it stands alone; a UUID-shaped string glued to a letter, a digit or a four-digit group (through one space or hyphen) is read by the rule, so `<uuid-shaped prefix><number>` and a UUID's last group joined to four more digits as a VID are found.
- **T / I (a product loosening the rule):** `allow` (`e164_in`: `+91` and ten digits, ungrouped, the `+` starting a token; `decimal`: an ungrouped run next to a `.` with a digit beyond it) is off by default in the library, the scan options, the `beforeDrop` sweep and the CLI (`--allow`); an unknown name is refused, so a typo cannot widen it. A product that turns one on accepts that an Aadhaar number written that way is missed (product guide §7dv). The AI scrubber and the e-KYC check do not use it.
- **R / availability:** `TestDatabase.end()` returns its first call's promise to every later or concurrent call, so the hook runs once and a second `end()` cannot race the drop or hide the hook's error.
- **What it still does not see:** named keys in files (the file sweep has no field structure; `findAadhaarNamedFields` is there for a product's own JSON), a text column holding JSON that does not parse, materialised views and foreign tables, and everything §3.138 lists.

### 3.167 The fake AA's FI payloads in STORAGE, joined to statement analysis (`FakeAccountAggregator.storage`, `fakeFiPayload`, `aaStatementAnalysisJoinCases`; PR-UDF-065, R1-D-243)

- **Scope:** a fake and contract cases, no adapter, port, setting or table. The new data flow is the fake AA (fixture environments and tests) writing synthetic FI payloads into the STORAGE it is given, where a STATEMENT_ANALYSIS adapter reads them, as `setu-aa` already writes real ones (§3.67, §3.144).
- **I (FI data in a weaker store):** each payload goes to the `REGULATED` class only, under an IPB-01-27 key in the fetch's `tenant/<tenant>/<module>/` prefix (checked before the write), so a product's fixture STORAGE keeps it under its REGULATED rules; the analysers refuse an input outside that class (`IPB-PORT-003` `residency`), which the join cases assert. The bytes are synthetic: a masked account, round figures, no identity number or 12-digit run (the cases sweep them with `findAadhaarLike`). The fake must not be wired where live FI data flows (as for every fake).
- **T (an altered statement analysed):** the payload's SHA-256 is computed on exactly the bytes stored, so the analyser's existing check binds the analysis to what the AA wrote; the join cases assert that a payload changed in place is refused `hash_mismatch` and a removed one `input_missing`, both before any analyser call. A key outside IPB-01-27 (the fake's old `<session>-<fiType>.json` shape included) is refused by the request schema.
- **E (another tenant's prefix):** as with `setu-aa`, the fake AA writes under the prefix the caller names and does not compare it with the call's tenant; the analysers refuse inputs and a report in two tenants and a call from another tenant (`tenant_mismatch`). **D:** at most one object per consented FI type per fetch. **Residual:** the default `MemoryStorage` of a fake built without `storage` holds payloads in memory for the fake's life.

### 3.168 The customer step-up (`CustomerIdentityPort.startStepUp`, `confirmStepUp`, `assertCustomerStepUp`, `customerStepUpChallenge`, `connectors/0017`; PR-UDF-056, R1-D-244)

- **S (a stolen or left-open session performing a sensitive act):** a product's sensitive borrower action (e.g. downloading an original document) checks `assertCustomerStepUp` against its declared policy (default: a fresh one-time code within 300 s); a session whose last authentication is older answers 401 `insufficient_user_authentication`, and only a fresh code to the account's **registered login** (checked with the IdP, `idpFindUser`) satisfies it. Someone holding the device but not the phone or mailbox cannot step up. A refresh never counts as an authentication, so a long-lived session cannot renew its age by refreshing.
- **S / E (stepping up one session from another, or carrying a step-up across sessions):** the start needs a DPoP proof by the key of the **active** device session named (read past the 15 s cache), and the confirmation the same key, the same account and the same device id; another device's key, another device's session, a key that is no signed-in device, or a session signed out between start and confirm is refused, before a try is counted. The step-up is recorded on that device row only: other devices keep their own `auth_time`, and a new sign-in of the same key is a new device id that starts from its own sign-in. A step-up challenge is never accepted as a sign-in, login-change or email code, nor they as a step-up (`purpose`).
- **T / I (codes):** the platform makes the six-digit code from the CSPRNG and keeps only its salted SHA-256 and the device id on the challenge (no code, no login); the code goes only through NOTIFY and is never stored, logged or returned. No new cryptographic primitive: the existing DPoP proof verification (C-11, R1-D-18) and SHA-256.
- **D / brute force:** the account's `auth.otp` guard applies unchanged and is shared with sign-in codes (10 codes per 10 minutes, the resend interval, 5 tries per code, back-off after 5 wrong codes, a 15-minute lockout after 10; `int.identity.customer.otp.*`). A refused start (bad proof, wrong device, foreign login) sends nothing and costs the IdP nothing.
- **I (the refusal as an oracle):** the 401 carries only the required level and max age (`required_acr`, `max_age`) and a fixed description; never the session's level, its age, or which condition failed. The staff `stepUpChallenge` ignores a customer refusal, so it is never rendered as a Bearer challenge.
- **T (clock and replica skew):** `auth_time` comes from the platform store's clock, not the device; one up to 30 s in the future counts as age 0, further ahead fails closed. On another replica the device cache may lag a step-up by up to 15 s, which fails closed (the borrower is asked again); `verifyRequest({ fresh: true })` on the sensitive route removes the lag and refuses a revoked device at once.
- **R:** the step-up is written with its ledger entry in one transaction (`identity.customer.device.step_up`: the device id, the level, the time, whether an active device was found), beside the existing entries for the send, the challenge and every try. `step_up_at` never moves back.
- **Storage:** two nullable INTERNAL columns on `connectors.customer_device` (`connectors/0017`, expand-only; `step_up_acr` checked to `otp`), under the table's forced RLS and existing grants; they go with the device row (unbinding, closure, 12-month retention). **Residual:** the step-up is session-wide, not per action (owner question #176 (4)); a borrower whose registered login is lost cannot step up until it is changed (PR-UDF-034); SMS delivery is as strong as the mobile network (IPB-02-02 keeps it off every staff check, and customer realms are separate).

### 3.169 Recording paid stamps and the NSDL eSign shell (`recordCertificate`, `shcil-estamp`, `digio-estamp`, `manual-paper`, `nsdl-esign`; PR-UDF-054, R1-D-245)

- **E (money moved through a stamp adapter):** none of the four can buy: every recording adapter refuses `purchase` with `purchase_not_permitted` before any check or request (tested: no request reaches a stub), the stubs have no purchase route, `digio-estamp` never orders or affixes from prepaid inventory, and `recordCertificate` takes no `MoneyAuthorisation` because nothing is bought.
- **S (fabricated or recycled stamp evidence):** an e-stamp is read back from its issuer and compared (number, State, duty, issue date; mismatch, unknown and cancelled certificates refused). A certificate number is recorded once per State and channel, so one stamp cannot evidence two instruments (the product's table needs the unique index). Manual paper cannot be confirmed by anyone outside: it is marked `MANUAL_RECORD`, carries the vendor's or franking agent's licence and the scan's document id, and whether a checker must confirm it before it enters a pack is the owner's (#177 (3)); an agent is never the recorder (`IPB-SEC-004`).
- **T (a record for another document):** the stamped document is read from STORAGE and its SHA-256 checked before any issuer is asked; a replayed key with another record is refused; a certificate attaches to one document. Duty below the stated duty is refused (`deficient_stamping`), as `PackStampEvidence` refuses it.
- **I (personal data to issuers):** SHCIL is sent the certificate number, State and issue date; Digio its own id; the manual adapter calls nothing (tested with a `fetch` spy). The purchaser's name is neither sent nor kept: only the party reference stays on the record. Duty and parties are REGULATED; all adapters are India-hosted.
- **R:** the adapter writes only the product's store; the product records the stamp inside its kernel mutation, so the hash-chained ledger entry is in the same transaction (X-02).
- **E, T (unapproved cryptography, NSDL):** the CCA eSign request must be signed by the ASP (XML-DSig, RSA); that is not an approved C-item, so the request is built unsigned and the fixed signer refuses (`step: asp_request_signature`); no option plugs in another signer and the adapter has no network path. Response verification and the signed PDF wait for the verify-only #162 verifier; no Aadhaar number passes through the adapter (the signer enters it at the ESP), and methods other than Aadhaar OTP and biometric are refused, never downgraded.
- **Residual:** every vendor shape is stub-shaped [VERIFY] until the owner's SHCIL, Digio e-stamp and NSDL accounts exist; all four adapters are off by setting and `unproven` (#177).

### 3.170 Verify-only PAdES/CMS signatures (`verifyPadesSignatures`, `CCA_INDIA_TRUST`; PR-UDF-059, C-28, owner answer #162, R1-D-246)

A product passes a PDF's bytes (and, optionally, CRLs and OCSP responses it fetched) and gets fixed labels back. Nothing is signed, fetched, stored or logged.

- **S (a forged or self-made signer):** trust comes only from `CCA_INDIA_TRUST`, PEM pinned in the source with SHA-256 fingerprints checked at load, changed only by PR, never downloaded. Every CA on the path must be pinned; certificates in the PDF serve only to find the signer. Each certificate signature is verified with an allowed algorithm (RSA ≥ 2048 PKCS#1 v1.5/PSS SHA-256/384/512, ECDSA P-256/SHA-256, P-384/SHA-384), CAs need `cA`, `keyCertSign` and their path length, unknown critical extensions fail the path, and the signer needs signing key usage. A test store (`customTrustStore`) is reported as `trustStore: "custom"`. **Residual:** the pin set is empty until the owner supplies CCA's certificates (#178), so nothing verifies as trusted today (fails closed).
- **T (a changed document):** `messageDigest` must equal the digest of the ByteRange, the ByteRange must start at 0 and leave out exactly the `/Contents` string, and the CMS must be followed only by zero padding. Bytes after the signature are an incremental update, reported per signature (`coversWholeDocument`) and at document level (`incremental_update_after_signature`); what such an update changes is not analysed (no DocMDP or field-lock check), so a product must treat that outcome as not valid. Signature dictionaries in compressed object streams are not found.
- **T (a weak algorithm):** SHA-1 and MD5 anywhere (digest, signature, certificate, CRL, OCSP, timestamp imprint) are `weak_algorithm`; the signature algorithm's hash must match the digest algorithm; every algorithm passes `assertApproved` (C-28 registry lines).
- **R (time and revocation):** a B-T timestamp is verified fully and chained to the same pinned store; without one the time is the signer's claim (`signingTime`, then `/M`), which a key holder could backdate (`timeSource` says which; #178 (4)). Revocation is only from caller-supplied, issuer-signed, current CRLs and OCSP responses, and any revocation is fatal; otherwise `not_checked`.
- **I (document content in results or logs):** outcomes are fixed labels; the only strings returned are the signer's certificate names, serial and fingerprint (the plan's output). Error messages inside the readers are fixed texts. Nothing is logged.
