- **D (hostile input):** file ≤ 32 MiB by default (64 MiB cap), ≤ 16 signatures, CMS ≤ 1 MiB, each object read once and at most 4 MiB, tokeniser depth 16 and 200,000 tokens, strings 64 KiB; DER: definite lengths only, minimal encodings, element-count caps, no recursion over input; CRLs ≤ 8 MiB and 2,000,000 entries, OCSP ≤ 64 KiB, at most 16 of each. Mutation, truncation, nesting and a large hostile object are tested to return labels within bounds and never throw.
- **E (scope creep):** the verifier modules have no signing call, private key, network module or SHA-1/MD5 hash (a test checks the source). No port, adapter, setting, table or migration.

### 3.171 PDF/A-2b and tagged PDF from `toPdf` (`pdfa`, `lang`, `pdfua`, `export/icc/`; `TEST_IMAGES.verapdf`; PR-FBX-52, R1-D-248)

Extends §3.98 and §3.99. Opt-in; without `pdfa` the bytes are 1.8.5's.

- **T (a tampered colour profile in the package):** the sRGB ICC profile is vendored with its upstream commit and SHA-256 (`export/icc/ICC.md`, CC0), published inside the signed and attested package, read only from there (never a caller's profile, never fetched) and embedded as is; `toPdf` never parses it. A unit test pins its SHA-256.
- **T, R (a statement that renders differently, or a date that lies):** PDF/A output is a pure function of the blocks, the options and `created`: no clock, no random value, no environment; the XMP dates equal /CreationDate and /ModDate, and the document and instance ids are the same content digest as /ID. A product can hash a statutory statement once and re-render it to prove the bytes (tested twice in one process, once in another, and under a moved system clock). The render time is never disclosed beyond the `created` the caller passes.
- **I (metadata in two places):** the title, the creator and the language now appear in the XMP packet as well as /Info, and link URLs in each annotation's `/Contents` as well as its URI. A step that strips or redacts /Info alone leaves them in the XMP: keep personal data out of `title` and `creator`, or strip both. No new field is added beyond what the caller passes.
- **T (content hidden from assistive technology or a validator):** every drawing operation is inside exactly one tagged or artifact sequence (unit test), the structure follows reading order, and only rules and repeated table headers are artifacts, so no caller text is marked as an artifact. `pdfua` refuses rather than claims a document without a title or with skipped heading levels.
- **E, S (the validator image):** veraPDF (`verapdf/cli:v1.30.3`, pinned by digest, pushed 27 Oct 2026, the veraPDF Consortium's image) runs in the `@infrabyx/export` integration suite only, with `--network none`, all capabilities dropped, `no-new-privileges`, a read-only root, and the PDFs mounted read-only; it carries a JVM (Temurin 11), started with the CVE-2026-114938 flag on `JAVA_OPTS` (R1-D-192; `JVM_IMAGES`, checked live in the `containers` job). It is GPL-3.0/MPL-2.0 and never shipped.
- **No new port, adapter, setting, table, migration, runtime dependency or algorithm** (SHA-256 as before, via `@infrabyx/crypto`).

### 3.172 Outbound partner calls over mTLS (`connectors` `egress/partner-mtls`, `egress/guard`; `crypto` `checkClientCredential`; PR-UDF-069, R1-D-250)

A product calls a partner (a lender's system) with the platform's client certificate. Data flow: product module → `PartnerMtlsClient.request` → SECRETS (the certificate and key, by reference) → DNS → the partner's host on 443. Outbound counterpart of §3.162.

- **S (talking to the wrong server):** TLS 1.2 or later with `rejectUnauthorized: true`; SNI and the certificate are checked against the configured host name, against the pinned `serverCa` when given (it replaces Node's roots) or Node's bundled roots. The socket is pinned to the address that was checked, so a second resolution cannot move it, and a redirect is never followed (any 3xx refused). **Residual:** no CRL or OCSP check of the partner's server certificate (Node's default); a partner whose CA is compromised is trusted until the pin changes (#180 (f)).
- **E, I (SSRF through a partner call):** the URL must be `https:` on 443, without user-info or an IP literal, to one of the client's exact hosts (configured, never user-supplied, no wildcard). Every DNS answer must be public (the `governed` ranges, now in `egress/guard.ts`: IANA special-purpose IPv4, multicast, 240/4, everything outside IPv6 `2000::/3` including IPv4-mapped, ULA and link-local); one refused answer refuses the call.
- **I (the client key):** read from SECRETS by reference at first use, checked in `@infrabyx/crypto` (P-256, matching the certificate, a client-auth leaf, valid now), then held only inside a TLS secure context for at most `credentialTtlMs` (5 minutes by default) and never past notAfter. The key is never logged, returned, put in an error or held by the product; `checkCredentials()` answers dates and `x5tS256` only. **Residual:** the key is in process memory while cached, and must be in SECRETS: a KMS-held key cannot sign a Node TLS handshake (#180 (b)).
- **I (data in errors and logs):** refusals carry a fixed detail and label, never the URL's path or query, a header value, the body, the SECRETS reference or the key (tested). The User-Agent names no tenant or deployment; no cookie is sent or kept.
- **T, R (a duplicated or unrecorded call):** `POST` and `PUT` only, each with a required `Idempotency-Key`; a timeout or abort says `maybeSent`, so a retry reuses the key and the partner de-duplicates. The ledger entry for the call is the product's, in its module's transaction.
- **D (a slow or hostile partner):** one deadline (15 s default, 120 s ceiling) over SECRETS, DNS, the handshake and the answer, plus the caller's `AbortSignal`; the request body is capped (1 MiB default), the answer is capped (1 MiB default) and the read aborted at the cap; identity encoding only, so no decompression.
- **E (cryptography scope):** no new primitive; the client key's TLS signature stays inside the approved `ecdsa-p256-sha256` entry (registry line added). RSA and P-384 client keys are refused until the owner widens it (#180 (a)). No setting, table or migration; `node:crypto` is imported only in `@infrabyx/crypto`.

### 3.173 ESIGN signer certificates, the fake's STORAGE writes and multi-document envelopes (`signerCertificate`, `padesSignerCertificates`, `createMultiDocumentEnvelope`; PR-UDF-070, R1-D-251)

Extends §3.170 and the ESIGN entries. A new data flow: the signer certificate's fields from the ESP's signed PDF into each signature record; the fake ESIGN's signed files and chains into STORAGE.

- **S, R (a record that names the wrong certificate):** an adapter fills `signerCertificate` only from a signature bound to that certificate by C-28's checks (ByteRange shape, CMS digest, the CMS signature over the signed attributes), and only when each bound signature maps to exactly one signer (envelope order when sequential; distinct ESP signing times when parallel); any doubt (a missing, unbound or extra signature, equal or missing times) leaves every record without one, never a guess. **Residual:** the summary names a certificate, it does not say the certificate is trusted: a product that relies on it must still run `verifyPadesSignatures` (whose CCA pins are empty until #178). The mapping assumes one ESP signature per signer in signing order ([VERIFY] per vendor).
- **T (a crafted PDF or certificate):** the readers are C-28's bounded ones (file ≤ 64 MiB here, ≤ 16 signatures, CMS ≤ 1 MiB, strict DER); `padesSignerCertificates` never throws for content and `signerCertificateSummary` throws one fixed-text `TypeError`. The common name is decoded per its ASN.1 string type, stripped of control characters and capped at 256 characters, so it cannot carry line breaks or control sequences into logs or UIs.
- **I (personal data):** the subject CN of an Aadhaar eSign certificate is usually the signer's name; with the issuer, serial and fingerprint it is signing evidence, CONFIDENTIAL like `signerIp`. Nothing is logged by the platform; products keep it with the signature record under their privacy notice. No Aadhaar number or other subject attribute (postal code, pseudonym, subject serial) is read.
- **E (new cryptography):** none. Field reading with `X509Certificate`, SHA-256 of the DER, and C-28's existing verification steps; no signing, key or network call (the C-28 source test covers the new module).
- **D, T (multi-document envelopes):** at most 10 documents, unique ids and storage keys, each checked against its SHA-256 by the adapter that reads it; completion is all or nothing (no signed document downloads before every document is signed by every signer, a decline declines all), so a product never acts on part of a set. Vendor adapters refuse it `unsupported` until verified.
- **T, I (the fake in a fixture environment):** `FakeEsign({ storage })` writes only under the document's own tenant prefix, as REGULATED, with IPB-01-27 keys and the SHA-256 of the bytes; its CA is made at run time (no key committed), lives only in the process and chains to nothing real; fakes run below staging only (R1-D-181).

### 3.177 The verified detached JWS beside the inbox event (`EcosystemInbox` `regulated`, `eco_inbox.jws`, `tenancy/0026`; PR-UDF-073, R1-D-257)

Extends §3.50 and §3.51 and closes §3.50's residual on G5 for products that pass the JWS to the inbox. New data flow: the `X-Infrabyx-JWS` value goes from the product's handler into `receive`, is verified there and stored in the event's row.

- **S, T (an unverified or forged proof stored as if verified):** `receive` stores a JWS only after `verifyRegulatedMessage` (C-22 profile: ES256 allow-list, exact header, the inbox's pinned key ring, the `iat` window, `jti` claimed last) accepts it over the parsed envelope that is stored with it; any failure is IPB-API-018 and nothing is stored or ledgered. Without the `regulated` option a `jws` is refused, never stored unverified. The envelope's own limits (`regulatedJwsFromHeaders`: one compact detached JWS, at most 1,024 octets) are checked first, and a CHECK constraint holds the same length and form below the application.
- **T, R (a proof replaced or erased after receipt):** the column is written once, at insert: a BEFORE UPDATE trigger refuses any change (42501), including null to a value and by the table's owner, so a stored proof always re-verifies against the stored envelope (key ring permitting) and an unsigned row cannot gain a proof later. Every receipt is ledgered with `signed` and `kid`.
- **R (a replay passed off as a redelivery):** a JWS whose `jti` was already claimed is accepted only as a `duplicate` of the very row that holds that exact JWS, looked up under the tenant's forced RLS; offered for another event it fails the signature, and in another tenant it stays a `replay` refusal. A redelivery with a fresh JWS must verify in full, and the answer carries the JWS stored first.
- **I, E (a tenant reading another's proofs):** the column lives in the module's existing `eco_inbox` table, under its forced RLS by tenant and its grants (no delete); class INTERNAL (the JWS holds `alg`, `kid`, `typ`, `iat`, `jti` and a signature, no personal data). The trigger and helper functions run with `search_path = pg_catalog`, are not executable by `public`, and the helper validates the schema name.
- **D:** the 0026 upgrade adds a nullable column without default and one CHECK to each existing inbox (a scan of a column that is all null) under the migration's 5 s lock and 60 s statement timeouts.
- **Residual:** the product must not verify the JWS itself before passing it (the `jti` would be claimed and the event refused as `replay`, failing safe), and still refuses unsigned regulated messages itself; rows received before 0026 have no JWS (nothing is backfilled); `OperationsHubProvisioning` keeps its own `proofs.save` path unchanged. No new port, adapter, setting, role, grant, algorithm or runtime dependency.

### 3.178 Shared document types, the executed-file link, `FakeDocgen` files and the PDF claim reader (`EXECUTED_DOCUMENT_TYPE`, `source_document`, `executedDocumentMismatches`, `FakeDocgenFiles`, `docgenStorageContractCases`, `pdfIdentification`, `pdfUaClaimed`; PR-UDF-077, R1-D-259)

Extends §3.171 and the §6.13 document flow.

- **S, T (an executed file swapped for another):** the binding of record is the signed `ESignCompleted` (`document_id` of the lender's document, `signed_sha256` of the file the ESP returned) and the executed file's `DocumentShared.sha256`, which its document JWS signs; a consumer verifies both signatures, then `executedDocumentMismatches`, then the downloaded bytes against `sha256`. `source_document` is not signed by the v2 document JWS, so it is a filing pointer that must agree, never proof; a mismatch is reported by a fixed label and never quotes either value.
- **S (a claim taken for conformance):** `pdfIdentification` reports what the catalog's XMP claims; a forged file can claim PDF/A or PDF/UA without meeting either. It is documented and named as a claim; acceptance that depends on conformance runs a validator (veraPDF, as the `@infrabyx/export` integration suite does). Metadata of pages, images or fonts never counts, so an embedded image cannot lend its claim to the document.
- **D (a hostile PDF):** the reader caps the file (64 MiB default, refused beyond), each inflate (4 MiB) and all inflates (32 MiB), reads at most 64 object streams, keeps object-stream offsets increasing, bounds every look-back to 64 KiB and processes only the last catalog match, so it stays linear (unit test on a file of repeated keys); anything it cannot follow is `{}`, and fuzzed truncations and byte flips never throw. Nothing is executed, fetched or opened: no actions, JavaScript, embedded files or annotations are read.
- **I (template data in STORAGE):** with files, `FakeDocgen` writes the data it renders (CONFIDENTIAL by default, as `RenderRequest.data` is) under the caller's tenant prefix in the bucket class given (default `CONFIDENTIAL`), as a real adapter does; a prefix that is not `tenant/<tenant>/<module>` is refused before anything is written. It is a fake for fixture environments, never above staging (guide §7x). The HTML page it writes escapes every value.
- **No new port, adapter, setting, table, migration, runtime dependency or algorithm** (SHA-256 via `@infrabyx/crypto`; inflate via `node:zlib`).

### 3.179 The ESP's signed file under the tenant's named key (`EsignDownloadOptions.keyRef`; PR-UDF-074, R1-D-260)

Extends §3.18, §3.173 and the named-key entries (R1-D-227, R1-D-240). A changed data flow: the signed PDF the ESP returns is written to STORAGE REGULATED under a reference the product names, instead of always under the class key.

- **I (a lender's signed agreement under the platform's key):** with `keyRef` the write is under that lender's key or not at all. A malformed reference (or a key id in disguise) is refused before the ESP is called; every STORAGE refusal, and a disabled or revoked key (`IPB-KMS-001` `storage_key_unavailable`), fails the download with nothing written, and the adapters never retry without the reference (tested with a store that refuses the named write). A product's `tenantKeyRefPrefixes` entry for `tenant/<tenant>/esign/` gives the same protection without naming the key, and a different named reference is then refused `key_ref_prefix_conflict`. **Residual:** a download that names no reference, outside such a prefix, is under the class key as before; a product that needs the lender's key must name it or map the prefix.
- **T (the ESP's bytes):** not scanned on this path: the file is the contracted ESP's answer over its authenticated API (as KYC, bureau, AA and e-stamp files), checked as a PDF by its magic bytes and read only by C-28's bounded parsers. IPB-02-44's scan binds uploads; a product that also wants the ESP's file scanned runs FILE_SCAN on the stored key, or writes to QUARANTINE and promotes with `promotedObjectMeta` (owner question #189 (1)).
- **E, R:** no new permission: the platform's STORAGE role already needs `kms:GenerateDataKey` on each lender key for that lender's other writes (the operator's key policy, unchanged). The reference, never the key id, is recorded on the object and readable in `get`'s `meta.keyRef`, so audit can show which key protects each signed file. No new cryptography.
- **D:** a lender key that is unavailable stops that lender's signed-file downloads (fail closed by request); the envelope stays `completed` at the ESP and the download can be repeated once the key is restored.

### 3.109 Tenant entitlements (`tenancy/0020`; PR-FBX-43, #123, R1-D-107)

- **E (a tenant licensing itself, or one person licensing a tenant):** a version is written only on the platform's approved path: the insert policy needs a platform context and the action `tenancy.entitlement.change`, the service checks two different platform people (requester PLT_SUPER_ADMIN or PLT_FINANCE; approver a different PLT_SUPER_ADMIN or PLT_SECURITY_OFFICER), refuses services, support roles, tenant users and impersonations, and the table refuses a row whose approver is its requester. R4 (60-minute delay) in production. **R:** versions and notices are insert-only; both steps are ledgered in the tenant. **D (a licence lapsing mid-work):** a lapse or revocation refuses writes only; every record stays readable until the product's detach workflow, and the tenant is told of each grant, revocation and lapse. **I:** the tenant reads only its own licences and notices (forced RLS); of the two SECURITY DEFINER functions, `entitlement_status` reads only the transaction's own tenant and `entitlement_lapses_due` (the sweep's cross-tenant list) returns nothing outside a platform context; both are on the RLS lint's reviewed list.
